# IRIS/CRISPEX plan review — full verified results (2026-09-27)
Generated from workflow wf_94b0a9c3-4f4 (46 agents). Findings were adversarially re-checked; 'refuted' ones are listed separately. Nothing in the repos was edited.

## High findings
### wp1-links-1 — Pixel WCSLink silently overrides link_hpc when both are present
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** When a WCSLink and link_hpc are both present, pixel-ROI propagation (raster ROI to SJI) always takes the 1-hop WCSLink, whatever order the links were added in. So the per-frame raster-to-SJI behaviour that D1 relies on is lost for pixel ROIs. Subsets on world coordinates still go through link_hpc, so 'silently overrides link_hpc' is too broad. Plan 571-572 already asks for a combined-graph test, but no WP1 checkbox (702-719) or prototype test puts both links in one collection, and the real browser flow (sources/iris.py:61-62) runs the autolinker dialog and then link_hpc.
- **Recommendation:** Add a WP1 policy for which pairs get which link. For example, the browser and link_iris could decline or remove WCSLink suggestions for SJI<->raster pairs that link_hpc covers. Add a combined-graph regression that runs the real autolinker instead of monkeypatching it away.
- **Evidence:** link_manager.py:54-94 (glue 1.27.0, identical in core-ape14) keeps the lowest-depth path. On core-ape14 (#2595 head c49aeb1 per gh) with WP1, d1_combined.py gives: 0.8.1 fixture wcs 0 / hpc 37020 px in 32 frames / both 0, 'both == wcs: True'; 0.9.0 fixture wcs 7254 / hpc 23374 / both 7254 in both orders. world_both.py: an SJI-lon world subset gives 122496 on the raster for hpc and for both, 'world subset both == hpc: True'. glue-qt application.py:1442-1445 calls run_autolinker from add_datasets. wp1 test_linking.py:172 monkeypatches it away.
### wp2-wp6-science-1 — Default rest wavelength is TWAVE, which shifts Mg II k velocities by +16 km/s; WP2 and WP6 disagree on this
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** WP2 fills in and falls back to TWAVE (plan:732; wp2_moments_module.py:66-69; test_wp2_moments.py:37 expects '2796.20'). WP5 falls back to irispy metadata (plan:847), while WP6 rejects TWAVE (plan:879; wp6_fitting.py:134-135). TWAVE is the Mg II k window's nominal wavelength (2796.20 Å) against the vacuum line at 2796.35 Å, so velocities are biased by about +16 km/s. One detail is wrong: the Si IV 1394 bias is about -4 to -5 km/s, not +4 (TWAVE 1393.78 is longer than the 1393.755-1393.76 line); TWAVE matches the line list for Si IV 1403, C II 1336 and O I 1356.
- **Recommendation:** Use one policy in WP2, WP5 and WP6: an explicit override, otherwise the WP5 line-list entry that lies in the window (nearest TWAVE), otherwise no velocity. Never use TWAVE silently as a Doppler reference. Show the chosen line in the dialog.
- **Evidence:** Installed irispy 0.8.1 meta.py:193-198 returns TWAVE{n}. My header scan of 10 local L2 rasters showed TWAVE 2796.19995117 / 1393.7800293 / 1402.77001953 / 1335.70996094 in every file. mom.py on the 2018-01-02 raster printed 'TWAVE 2796.200, vacuum 2796.352; ... +16.30 km/s' and 'Si IV 1394 ... -5.38 km/s'. A single-Gaussian fit on that raster gave median centroid 2796.345 Å, which matches vacuum k. git show v0.8.1:examples/analysis/07_mg_ii_two_gaussian_fitting.py:129 has mg_ii_core = 279.6351 nm, and iris_lines.csv has 2796.35.
### wp2-wp6-science-2 — WP2 default 'blank wings = whole window' gives meaningless moments on real IRIS windows
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Plan:733 makes blank wings mean the whole window, and the prototype passes wings=None. On real full-size windows that holds several lines, the dialog defaults give meaningless moments. With ±0.5 Å wings around the vacuum rest, velocities come back near zero. irispy zeroes negative and non-finite samples (moments.py:135), which leaves a noise pedestal. For faint lines this inflates sigma even when wings are set.
- **Recommendation:** Require wings by default, for example ±0.5 Å or ±100 km/s around the chosen line-list rest wavelength. Move 'prefilled wings from a profile range' (plan line 1022) into v1. Document the clipping bias and suggest a min_intensity threshold.
- **Evidence:** mom.py (2018 raster) printed these defaults: Mg II median v +504.5 km/s, sigma 5.879 Å (630.3 km/s); Si IV 1403 -63.1; C II +39.3. With ±0.5 Å vacuum-rest wings it printed -0.5, +2.6 and +0.6 km/s. The windows (2790.65-2809.95, 1398.12-1406.67, 1331.77-1340.44 Å) contain k+h, O IV 1399.77/1401.16 and C II 1334.53/1335.71 (iris_lines.csv). synth.py (seeds 1 and 2) printed a clipped whole-window sigma of 0.243/0.245 and a ±0.3 Å sigma of 0.110/0.112, against a true 0.060.
### wp3-wp5-1 — Default rest wavelength is the nominal window TWAVE, which biases Doppler velocities
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The plan (IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md:846-849) makes irispy metadata the default rest wavelength. irispy's SGMeta.rest_wavelength is just TWAVEn, in both 0.8.1 and 0.9.0. wp5_units.py:21 and :37 offer km/s whenever that value exists, and the WP2 dialog prefills it (wp2_moments_module.py:67). This gives a -16.3 km/s zero point for Mg II k and offers km/s for continuum windows (1343, 2832, 2826, 2814), which contradicts plan lines 450-451 and 879. The bias depends on the window: TWAVE equals the lab value for C II 1336, O I 1356 and Si IV 1403.
- **Recommendation:** Use this priority: explicit override, then the lab value from the packaged line list matched to TDESC, then unavailable. When a window holds several lines (C II 1334/1335, Mg II k/h), require the user to choose. Never offer km/s for continuum windows. Show the rest value in use. Apply the same rule to the WP2 prefill.
- **Evidence:** The installed irispy meta.py:194-198 returns float(TWAVE{_iwin}); `git show v0.9.0:irispy/meta.py` is still TWAVE-based. A header probe of the 2014 raster printed 'Mg II k 2796 2796.19995117', '2832 2832.70', '1343 1343.29', and '(2796.19995-2796.352)/2796.352*c = -16.30 km/s'. The archived plan measured the same effect (archive/2026-09-05/IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md:3433-3446, 'shift -16.07 km/s').
### wp3-wp5-2 — Sessions fail to restore when a Profile viewer uses a non-default x axis with a non-native display unit
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** This is broader than reported. Any Profile viewer whose x_att is not the default axis fails GlueApplication.restore_session with ValueError, even with the native unit ('m' on a synthetic cube, 'Angstrom' on an IRIS raster). The cause is that x_att and x_display_unit share restore priority 1 (glue/viewers/profile/state.py:333-341), and update_from_dict only delays callbacks (core/state_objects.py:101-109). So every IRIS wavelength-spectrum panel, the core CRISPEX view, is unrestorable, and the 1D-only tests (test_line_positions.py:41-63, wp5_ll_session.py) cannot catch it.
- **Recommendation:** Add a WP0/core item: give x_att a higher restore priority than x_display_unit, or validate after the group is set. Make multi-axis IRIS Profile save/restore with km/s and nm an acceptance test in WP3 and WP5, and add a gated workaround if needed.
- **Evidence:** prof_restore2.py (synthetic WAVE/HPLT/HPLN cube, stock 1.27.0) printed 'm RESTORE FAILED ValueError value m is not in valid choices: ['deg', ...]'. The same failure appears with PYTHONPATH=core-profile:qt-profile. iris_restore3.py (IRIS Si IV 1403 raster + wp3 + wp5) printed 'RESTORE FAILED ValueError value Angstrom is not in valid choices: ['arcsec', 'arcmin', 'deg']', and the same for nm and km / s. _update_priority is identical on glue upstream main (gh api), origin/main, profile-slice and profile-wcsaxes.
### wp3-wp5-8 — WP4's full-shape nearest-index components bloat file-referencing sessions
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Nearest-index components added by link_time have no LoadLog, so file-referencing sessions embed the full broadcast int32 arrays at about 5.3 bytes per pixel after base64. That is 18x on the tiny fixture pair and scales to GB for real SJI/raster sizes. This would stop the milestone's 'reopened session' on a representative dataset unless the indices are stored compactly. Severity is raised to high on that basis; the fix itself is local.
- **Recommendation:** Store compact 1D index arrays (for example a Component subclass with its own __gluestate__ that re-broadcasts on load, or recompute from Time on restore). Add a real-size session budget to the WP3/WP4 tests.
- **Evidence:** size_probe.py (SJI 1330 (52,40,37) + sns C II 1336 raster (187,40,18), wp3 factories) printed 'file-referencing session bytes before/after link_time: 66525 1197291'. The added 1,130,766 B over 211,600 px is about 5.34 B/px, consistent with int32 plus base64. The reviewer's 67,440 to 1,715,976 came from a different window. The embedded-mode Time/mask sizes were not re-checked.
### wp4-quicklook-1 — WP4 time links break the SJI Pixel -> raster marker/spectrum path the plan says to preserve
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** On core #2601+#2595 sources, adding link_time's identity ComponentLinks correlates SJI frame with raster step in pixel_cid_to_pixel_cid_matrix. PixelSubsetState's backward check then fails, so an SJI Pixel point loses its linked raster marker and spectrum. The raster-to-SJI Pixel direction still works. The plan never warns about this, even though it says to preserve the path (plan:564, 815) and mandates these links (plan:656-659).
- **Recommendation:** Do not register nearest-index maps as glue links. Keep them as plain per-frame arrays that the sync controller reads directly, or add links only if a regression test proves the Pixel path still works. Revise D-contract plan:656-659 to match.
- **Evidence:** Verifier probe scratchpad/verify-wp4-quicklook/test_v_links.py via the runner (core-combined:qt-profile:wp1:protos). Output: ('wcs','hpc') matrix [[0,0,0],[1,1,0],[1,1,0]] pixel (25,25) -> ((24,3),(1,1,29)); ('wcs','hpc','time') and ('time','wcs','hpc') give matrix [[1,0,0],[1,1,0],[1,1,0]] pixel (25,25) -> IncompatibleAttribute; raster pixel (10,20) -> SJI xy (35,21) in all three orders. Logic is at core-combined glue/viewers/image/pixel_selection_subset_state.py:36-45.
### wp4-quicklook-2 — Nearest-time link result depends on link order when #2595 WCS links exist
*prototype_bug, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** With WCS, hpc and time links together, resolving sji[raster step pixel cid, point] (the path sync uses at wp4_common.py:126) sometimes goes through the spatial WCS link and returns wrong steps. The claimed rule (WCS added first means the time link wins) is wrong. The outcome varies with insertion order and also between runs with the same order (hash/identity dependent). Sync must not rely on the link graph for nearest-time indices.
- **Recommendation:** Sync must read the precomputed nearest arrays explicitly, never resolve `data[other_pixel_cid, point]` through the link graph. Add an order-permutation test with WCS + hpc + time links.
- **Evidence:** test_v_links.py::test_order, all 6 permutations. Unseeded run: (wcs,hpc,time) 123 mismatches, e.g. (0,(25,25),2.976,0); (time,wcs,hpc) 123; (hpc,wcs,time) 0. PYTHONHASHSEED=0..3 runs: (wcs,hpc,time) 0 and (time,wcs,hpc) 0 under all four seeds; (wcs,time,hpc) and (hpc,wcs,time) 123; (hpc,time,wcs) flips between seeds 0 and 1. The back direction was not re-checked.
### wp7-wp8-1 — WP7 AIA crop fails for any IRIS field of view with an off-limb corner
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The finding holds, and the failure is broader than stated. Off-limb corners become NaN whenever the AIA frame's observer or obstime differs from the IRIS Earth frame at STARTOBS, which is always true for real AIA data. With an Earth observer and only a +5 s obstime change, the corners at 1000 arcsec are already NaN and submap raises ValueError. The brief (plan 928-931) prescribes only propagate_with_solar_surface. The synthetic test uses the same observer and obstime (test_context.py:41-46), so it hides the bug. The error is caught as a message box (context_chk.py:166-171), but limb and prominence pointings cannot get AIA context.
- **Recommendation:** In the brief, require `with propagate_with_solar_surface(), SphericalScreen(iris_observer, only_off_disk=True):` around the transform. Add a test that uses an off-limb FOV and an AIA frame whose observer is not Earth.
- **Evidence:** Scratch limb.py (sunpy 8.0.0), corners Tx=[700,900,1000,1000]. 'earth same time' gives propagate [700 900 1000 1000] and submap ok. 'earth +5s' gives [700.01 900 nan nan] and submap ValueError '...contain NaN values...'. 'sdo +5s' gives propagate [700.17 900.21 nan nan], and adding SphericalScreen(only_off_disk=True) gives [700.17 900.21 1000.24 1000.24]. See context_chk.py:106-110 and test_context.py:41-46.
### archive-trace-1 — Session-size blow-up from post-load broadcast components was dropped
*dropped_from_archive, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Archived WP4 pitfall 9 (archive/2026-09-05/IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md:3156) is not carried forward. The current plan requires precomputed nearest-index components (plan 656-659, 793), but WP3 (757-777) and WP4 (779-828) say nothing about their size in a session. Released core embeds every post-load component in full even with include_data=False. WP4's `Slit x` component (wp4_common.py:28) is also broadcast and added after loading. The WP6 residual (885-887) is also embedded, but it is a real array whose size matches the data, not a broadcast blow-up, so 'same problem' overstates it.
- **Recommendation:** Add a WP3/WP4 requirement: do not save post-load derived index components and re-derive them on restore, or store them compactly (1-D per axis). Also decide how the WP6 residual is saved. Add a session-size check on a representative SJI to the first-milestone 'reopened session' criterion (452-456).
- **Evidence:** My runner run (scratch test_vat_baseline.py, HOME=scratch/home1) printed 'SIZE baseline 1774 broadcast 21335469 real bytes 400 logical 16000000' for one int32 (100,200,200) broadcast component with include_data=False. .venv glue/core/state.py:1096-1100: only a component with `_load_log` is saved by reference. data_factories/helpers.py:282-310: LoadLog tags only the components that exist at load time. wp4_common.py:68,71 add the Nearest components after loading. Grep of the plan: no 'broadcast'/'size' text in WP3/WP4. The only size check, at 772, covers sessions without WP4 components.
### followup-1-1 — WP4 time links on pixel component IDs cannot share one graph with #2595 WCSLinks and link_hpc
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The prototype's identity links into pixel IDs (wp4_common.py:69, :72) break the SJI Pixel-to-raster marker and spectrum path (a) and the SJI-to-raster overlay (e) in 6 of 6 add orders once #2595 WCSLink and link_hpc are present, on irispy 0.8.1 and 0.9.0. Sync (c) and a baseline-equal raster-map ROI (b) are never both correct, because raster pixel 0 has one winning link. Design B (index links to plain 'Exposure index'/'Frame index' components) passes (a)-(e) in 6/6. Framing note: plan:656-659 alone is compatible with B; the defect is the prototype, which the WP4 brief tells implementers to 'Port' (plan:793-794), so the plan needs the explicit 'never target a pixel component ID' rule.
- **Recommendation:** Adopt design B, which passed (a)-(e) in 6/6 orders on irispy 0.8.1 and 0.9.0. Keep the nearest-index components, but link them to new plain index components instead of pixel IDs: SJI 'Nearest exposure: <raster>' to raster 'Exposure index', and raster 'Nearest frame: <sji>' to SJI 'Frame index'. Change sync_slices (wp4_common.py:122-126) to read those components instead of other.pixel_component_ids. Rewrite plan:656-660 to say 'never target a pixel component ID with a time link'.
- **Evidence:** My re-run (core-combined + WP1 pkg): r081.log P rows a=False e=False 6/6, with c passing only in hpc,time,wcs (ras_map->sji 450 px/3 frames) and 123/124 fwd mismatches elsewhere; r090.log (irispy v0.9.0 git archive) P a/e fail 6/6, B all True 6/6. My own test_indep.py: under P, pixel_cid_to_pixel_cid_matrix row 0 becomes [1,0,0] and back-dims [0,1,2]≠[1,2], giving IncompatibleAttribute (pixel_selection_subset_state.py:36-45, 57). The real Pixel tool builds the same slices (pixel_selection_mode.py:56-60).
### followup-1-2 — Pixel-targeting time links crash image layers with an uncaught Exception
*prototype_bug, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Pixel-targeting time links make translate_pixel raise a bare Exception, and ImageLayerArtist does not catch it. But the Exception is not raised out of viewer.add_data: add_data returns normally and the layer shows as enabled. The error comes from every canvas draw (layer_artist.py:140, then state.py:461, then fixed_resolution_buffer.py:86). It also happens on released glue-core 1.27.0 with the WP4 prototype configuration (link_hpc + link_time), so it does not depend on #2595.
- **Recommendation:** Add a WP4 rule: no link whose inputs are main components may target a pixel component ID. Optionally propose upstream that translate_pixel raise IncompatibleAttribute instead of a bare Exception. Add an overlay regression test (SJI layer in the raster viewer, and the reverse).
- **Evidence:** test_indep.py::test_qt_which_call on core-combined: 'add_data returned normally; sji layer visible: True enabled: True' then 'draw raised Exception Dependency on non-pixel component [... layer_artist.py:140:get_image_data, state.py:461:get_sliced_data, ... fixed_resolution_buffer.py:86:translate_pixel]'. On 1.27.0 the same run gives the same trace (state.py:460). The prior probe's try block wrapped add_data, visible and draw together. The except clause at core-combined layer_artist.py:141 lists only IncompatibleDataException, IncompatibleAttribute and IndexError; cmp shows the file is identical to 1.27.0. Design B re-run: 'Cannot visualize this layer'. Raster/SJI overlays are a CRISPEX reference behaviour (plan:410).
### followup-2-1 — Quicklook on real data crashes in 3 of 6 runs: two threads share the irispy -TAB WCS
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The crash is real, and it is worse than the finding says: it is a bug in the shipped solar main loader, not only in the prototype. Any stock Image viewer (WCSAxes, GUI thread) plus a Profile viewer on a raster over 1e7 elements (glue-qt worker thread) can crash on the shared irispy -TAB FITS WCS. Two details are wrong: the logs show 2 crashes in 6 runs (2 of the 5 runs that had Pixel clicks), not 3, and s4_session_si.txt skipped clicks and passed, so its line 19 shows no Bus error. The plan has no thread-safety item, and the WP6 worker also calls pixel_to_world_values (wp6_fitting.py:32,101,154).
- **Recommendation:** WP1: guard _GlueWCS pixel_to_world_values, world_to_pixel_values and axis_correlation_matrix with a per-instance threading.Lock, or give worker threads a deepcopy of the WCS. WP6's worker also calls wavelength_angstrom -> pixel_to_world_values (wp6_fitting.py:101,32,154). WP4 acceptance: 0 crashes in 20 consecutive offscreen quicklook runs of 3 Pixel clicks each on a window with more than 1e7 elements.
- **Evidence:** glue-qt layer_artist.py:50 threads the profile when layer.size > 1e7. My solar-main probe (verify-followup-2/test_main_race.py: stock viewers plus solar-main glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py, slider redraw while the profile computes) crashed 3/3: exit 138/133/138, main thread in iris.py:53 -> all_pix2world, worker in fitswcs axis_correlation_matrix. Rerunning scale/wcs_threads.py gave tab exit 133/138/139 and plain 0/0. Adding a threading.Lock around both threads' calls gave 3/3 'NO CRASH' (verify-followup-2/wcs_threads_lock.py). astropy#19174 is OPEN (gh issue view).
### followup-2-2 — Loader stores masks as int64 (2x the data) and the stack keeps a redundant in-RAM mask
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Every IRIS mask component, stacked or not, is stored as int64 (8 B per element, twice the float32 data): Component() calls coerce_numeric, which does bool.astype(int). The stack also rebuilds mask = ~isfinite(output). The plan does not treat the stack mask as undocumented: WP9 (line 996) already says 'NaN/mask reconstruction', and solar main tests assert that the stack mask component exists (test_importer.py:171-180). Storing it as uint8 keeps that contract, while dropping it breaks a tested behavior. The 19.6 GB figure for all windows is a linear extrapolation that was not run.
- **Recommendation:** WP1: drop the stack mask component, because NaN already encodes it; if a mask is needed, use a derived component. Store unstacked masks as uint8/int8, which coerce_numeric leaves unchanged. Thresholds: retained RSS at most 6 B/element and peak at most 10 B/element after raster_data; all 7 windows of the 99-scan raster stacked under 12 GB peak on a 24 GB machine. If this holds, Dask can stay deferred for these datasets.
- **Evidence:** glue/core/component.py:53 calls coerce_numeric. glue/utils/array.py:158-160 converts bool to int (also at origin/main dae530c3). I observed Component(bool).dtype = int64 and Component(uint8).dtype = uint8. The loader adds the mask at iris.py:79-80@plan and the stack builds it at stack_spectrograms.py:79. s1_mg_stack.txt shows maxrss 9,696,706,560 B with memmapped=1.86 GB and masks=3.71 GB; s1_all_stack_25/50 show 5.22/10.07 GB. Note: I ran 'git -C ~/Git/glue fetch -q', which touches only remote-tracking refs.
### followup-2-3 — WP4 nearest-index components make 'file-referencing' sessions 5,400x larger
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** link_time adds Nearest components as broadcast views, which have no LoadLog entry, so glue's Component saver writes them out in full. With WP4 links, the file-referencing session grows from 235 KB to 1.28 GB on real data. On the fixtures it grows from 67 KB to 1.7 MB, which already exceeds WP3's 'under 100 KB' target, so fixture tests could catch this. The 3.6 GB and 8.6 GB session sizes (and the 9-window figure) are extrapolations; the 9-window number does not appear in any log.
- **Recommendation:** WP4: persist only the 1-D per-exposure/per-frame index vectors, for example as a small index dataset joined by pixel links, or recompute them from Time on restore through a custom saver. WP3 threshold: the quicklook session (SJI plus 2 windows plus WP4 links) is at most 1 MB and saves in at most 2 s.
- **Evidence:** wp4_common.py:68,71 add the components with np.broadcast_to. glue/core/state.py:1096-1101 saves context.do(component.data) when there is no _load_log. s4_session_si.txt shows 'SESSION with WP4 components: 1277702953 bytes in 5.1s', 'without ... 235111 bytes' and peak footprint 13149137216. I reran find-wp3-wp5/size_probe.py and got '67440 1715976'. Record sizes are consistent with int32 arrays of shape (1600,417,262) and (400,417,388) after np.save plus base64.
### followup-3-1 — Intermittent hard crash while stepping time with Profile + Image viewers of one IRIS raster (no owner)
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** With threaded profiles, test_12 (sit-and-stare Si IV, 2 Image viewers + 1 Profile viewer, slice set 100→300) crashes the process intermittently. It crashed in 4 of 8 fresh threaded runs (1 SIGBUS, 3 SIGTRAP) and in 0 of 4 runs with ComputeWorker.start disabled. The plan has no thread-safety item (plan:452-456; grep for thread/crash/race finds only WP6/WP8 worker text). A WCS race is plausible but not proven: in my SIGBUS the main thread was in wcsp2s while the workers were in numpy, not in wcs.py, so the _GlueWCS lock is an untested mitigation.
- **Recommendation:** Add a WP4/WP1 robustness task: serialize _GlueWCS pixel/world calls with a lock in glue-solar (D2), or give worker threads their own WCS copy. Add a threaded playback stress test (profile + image viewers, many slice steps) to the first-milestone gate and report the race upstream. A lighter stress test (scan-0 raster, 60 steps) did not reproduce the crash in 3 runs, so gate on the sit-and-stare scenario.
- **Evidence:** V=scratchpad/verify-followup-3: $V/rc.txt ('threaded 1 rc=138', '2/4/5 rc=133', '3/6/7/8 rc=0', 'sync 1-4 rc=0'). $V/log_t12_1.txt:19-24 has the main thread in wcs.py:1610 <- glue_solar iris.py:53, and at :87 in _wcs.abi3.so wcsp2s. In the same log, :6-17 has the ComputeWorkers in numpy _broadcast_to/ones <- profile/layer_artist.py:54. The worker calls WCS through data[x_att] (glue/viewers/profile/state.py:478-479), and _wcs.abi3.so imports _PyEval_SaveThread. Stale evidence: crispex-replay/log_i.txt now shows '16 passed, 11 xfailed', and only 3 synced logs exist, not 6. git status was clean after the runs.
### followup-4-1 — WP4 time linking fails on every negative-step (STEPS_AV<-0.01) raster
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** For STEPS_AV<-0.01 rasters, irispy flips the data and the times together, so the Time component correctly descends along the flipped step axis. wp4_common.nearest (lines 43-44) rejects an unsorted reference, so link_time (line 62) raises for a single scan and for a 2-scan stack of 3400109360, while a positive-step scan links fine. gwcs_raster_clean also reverses the times, so upgrading irispy does not help. The fix belongs in `nearest`: argsort the reference and map the indices back.
- **Recommendation:** In `nearest`, argsort the reference, search in the sorted copy and return the original indices. Keep the NaT/empty checks. WP4 acceptance test: link_time on a 3400109360 scan and a 2-scan stack must not raise, and matched offsets must be under half the cadence.
- **Evidence:** My probe (WP1 + released core 1.27.0): neg-scan descending=true, link_time 'ValueError: times must be finite and reference times sorted'; neg-stack same error; pos-scan (4000005156) 'ok'. File order: XCENIX -907.889 to -970.733 and TIME ascending. Code: .venv irispy/io/spectrograph.py:158 (v34) and :259 (times=t_obs[::-1]); `git -C ~/Git/irispy show gwcs_raster_clean:irispy/io/spectrograph.py` :149 times=times[::-1]. Plan line 800 records 'rejects empty, unsorted or NaT references' as the intended behaviour.
### followup-4-2 — Masks, 4D-stack NaNs and moments are wrong for negative-step rasters
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** irispy 0.8.1, v0.9.0 and upstream LM-SAL main (34b5805, 2026-09-21) build the mask from the unflipped data and flip only the data. They also leave per-step meta unflipped (exposure time, FOV centre, OBS_VRIX, OPHASEIX). As a result the per-scan mask, the stack's NaN positions and the WP2 moments are all wrong for negative-step rasters. The error in the moments comes from the mask alone: rerunning line_moments with the mask replaced by data==-200 removes it.
- **Recommendation:** Add a version-gated loader workaround (flip the mask when STEPS_AV<-0.01 and irispy lacks the fix), or require a fixed irispy. WP1/WP2 acceptance tests on 3400109360: mask equals data==-200 per scan; stack NaN positions equal the flipped raw -200 values; moments match a numpy reference.
- **Evidence:** My probe: irispy cube mask_eq_raw_bad=true, mask_eq_data_bad=false, mismatch=7026; meta fov Tx first=-907.889 while WCS step0 lon=-0.26965 deg (-970.73"). Stack: scan0 nan_valid=3513 and kept_m200=3513; scan1 58509. Moments with loaded vs corrected mask differ in 2987 map pixels: velocity 85.53 km/s, centroid 0.381 A, width 0.193 A, intensity rel 0.0888. Code: spectrograph.py:251,260,271,228-229; `gh api repos/LM-SAL/irispy/contents/irispy/io/spectrograph.py?ref=main` shows the same lines 251/260/271; gwcs_raster_clean:269-270 flips the mask. The plan has no STEPS_AV/V34 mention (grep).

## Medium findings
### core-qt-capabilities-1 — #2595 autolinks the time axis between two SJI channels, and a Pixel point then stops propagating between them
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** With core #2595 (core-combined), SJI 1400 and SJI 2796 from the 2021 fixture get one WCSLink over all three pixel axes, time included. The link maps frame 0 to NaN, and a PixelSubsetState on 1400 raises IncompatibleAttribute on 2796, so no marker or light curve appears there. Spatial ROIs still propagate. Released 1.27.0 suggests 0 links for this pair. So roadmap line 1043 ('time axes are still not autolinked') is wrong once #2595 lands. The WCS matrix (548-554) and the WP1 test list (717) have no SJI/SJI case.
- **Recommendation:** Add SJI/SJI (multi-channel) pairs to the WCS matrix and to the WP1 tests. Correct the roadmap row. In WP4, decide whether SJI/SJI links should be spatial-only so Pixel markers and light curves propagate, and whether the WCS time link or the nearest-index link is authoritative.
- **Evidence:** My probe t/test_v1_sji.py via run_checks with core-combined:qt-profile:solar and with :IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/wp1 gave the same result in both: 'nlinks 1 axes1 [0, 1, 2]', 'matrix [[1,1,1],[1,1,1],[1,1,1]]', 'mapped ... 0: [nan, 9.97, 19.97]', 't0 ... dt 9230 milliseconds', 'point lc FAILED IncompatibleAttribute', 'roi on b 6100 91760'. The released core with solar gave 'nlinks 0'. The failure comes from core-combined pixel_selection_subset_state.py:45-57 (dimension check, then raise). PR heads #2595 c49aeb1 and #2601 acaf4e1 are unchanged on GitHub.
### core-qt-capabilities-2 — Intersecting a Pixel subset with a band breaks linked extraction and hides the marker
*plan_incorrect, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The plan's band recipe (lines 488 and 515) is about the same dataset, and there it works: Pixel & band gives the band-restricted spectrum. The plan never says that AND-ed subsets work across linked data, so 'plan_incorrect' overstates the problem. The limitation itself is real and not documented. A Pixel & band AndState gives an all-NaN profile on a WCS-linked dataset. The Pixel crosshair marker also disappears once the subset is any AndState, even on the same dataset. Both apply to released 1.27.0 and to core-combined.
- **Recommendation:** State this limitation in the time-wavelength recipe and in WP4. When a WP4 preset adds band or scan filters, compute the band on the target dataset and keep the Pixel subset bare, or intersect after translating to the target's pixels. Test the marker and linked spectra with filters active.
- **Evidence:** My probe t/test_v2_band.py, on released core with glue-qt and on core-combined with qt-profile. Output: 'bare linked [660..710]', 'pt & band(b) linked [nan x6]', 'pt & band(a) same [nan 37. 38. nan nan nan]', 'marker bare True', 'marker anded False AndState'. Code in released core: data.py:1733-1735 (the SliceSubsetState fast path is taken only for a bare state), subset.py:1371-1373 (a non-aligned dataset returns broadcast False), viewers/image/layer_artist.py:319-331 (the marker draws only for isinstance PixelSubsetState).
### core-qt-capabilities-3 — Path slicer: core ships a complete tool and upstream Qt PR #66 is not tracked
*plan_stale, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** PR #2579 (merged 2026-05-22, shipped in v1.26.0 and 1.27.0) did add BasePathSlicerMode (multi-trace) and BasePathSlicerCrosshairMode to core, but core's own mode keeps the ndim==3 gate. Draft glue-qt #66 (head 0797f62) replaces and deletes the legacy pv_slicer/standalone viewer. Its new Qt module is 86 lines including a dropdown menu, not 'about 20'. The plan does not track it, and line 617 plans extensions to 'the existing 3D Qt slit tool' that #66 deletes. PathSlicedData does slice the real IRIS stack, but only the wavelength axis carries a physical world unit; Offset and Scan are unitless.
- **Recommendation:** Track glue-qt #66 as an external dependency. Rescope the 4D slit item as a glue-solar viewer_tool subclass of BasePathSlicerMode (per decision D2) with a 4D gate, per-axis parent driving and a PathSlicedData session saver. Do not extend the legacy PVSliceWidget.
- **Evidence:** core 1.27.0 path_slicer/matplotlib_mode.py:32 (class), :84 'enabled = ... ndim == 3', :130 (crosshair class); common.py:40-42 writes int(slice_y) to every non-displayed axis; grep finds no gluestate or saver code in path_slicer/. gh pr view 66: draft=true, astrofrog, 2026-05-27, +336/-608, deletes pv_slicer.py. glue-qt main 9780eaf ships only tools/pv_slicer. My probe t/test_v3_path.py printed 'stack (3, 8, 109, 17)', 'psd (3, 17, 11) finite 270' and "names ['Offset', 'Wavelength', 'Scan'] ['', 'm', '']". grep of the plan for #66, BasePathSlicer and 2579 finds nothing.
### core-qt-capabilities-5 — The fixed-layout tab registry does not work with app.viewers and is not restored from sessions
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** This is confirmed and slightly worse than reported. A plain QWidget, as in the docs example, cannot even be added: add_fixed_layout_tab connects tab.subWindowActivated and raises AttributeError. A widget that defines that signal is added, but app.viewers then fails (it is reported as a missing attribute) and _update_viewer_in_focus raises on activeSubWindow. With a subWindowList shim the session saves, but restore rebuilds only GlueMdiArea tabs, so the fixed layout is lost. The docs label the feature experimental.
- **Recommendation:** For WP4, prefer an MDI tab with tiled or explicit viewer positions, or require the fixed tab to emulate the QMdiArea API. Add a WP3 check that a restored preset keeps its arrangement, or accept MDI placement explicitly.
- **Evidence:** My probe t/test_v5_fixed.py on Qt upstream/main (identical application.py) and on the local glue-qt checkout gave: "plain add FAILED AttributeError 'Plain' object has no attribute 'subWindowActivated'", "viewers FAILED AttributeError", "focus FAILED ... 'Grid' object has no attribute 'activeSubWindow'", "restored tabs ['GlueMdiArea', 'GlueMdiArea'] [0, 1]". Source: upstream/main application.py:971-985, 1328-1342 (viewers iterates subWindowList), 1453-1469 (restore uses new_tab/add_widget); customization.rst:366 'still experimental'. profile_viewer_tool.py iterates session.application.viewers.
### upstream-state-1 — irispy gwcs_raster_clean branch breaks the glue-solar loader, and the plan does not track it
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The plan has no entry for irispy's gwcs_raster_clean work. On that branch, the unchanged glue-solar loader splits one raster file into 1496 2D datasets, loses wavelength from the world components and raises TypeError with stack=True. The branch is the user's own (all commits by Nabil Freij, last one 2026-09-08). It is pushed to LM-SAL/irispy but has no open PR, and irispy main instead merged FITS-TAB (#159). The risk is therefore real but depends on that branch landing; nothing breaks today. One small error in the recommendation: Tabular2D is used for time on this branch, and pointing comes from `_RasterSequenceCelestialTransform`.
- **Recommendation:** Add an irispy-compatibility gate to WP0/WP1: pin or CI-test against irispy main, and have `_raster_collection_data` accept both a sequence and a combined cube. Have `_GlueWCS` project out the extra time/step world axes, or wait for glue #2574. Note that irispy's per-scan Tabular2D pointing would supersede `stack_spectrogram_sequence` and the deferred 'per-scan absolute WCS' item (plan line 1014).
- **Evidence:** My own probe (scratch verify-upstream-state/probe.py, irispy exported from gwcs_raster_clean): 'single values {C II 1336: (SpectrogramCube, (187, 40, 18))}', 'single n datasets 1496 ... (40, 18) pix 2 world 3 ... world comps [Helioprojective Latitude, Helioprojective Longitude]', 'multi-noStack ... pix 3 world 5 ... world comps [Raster_Step, Seconds From Start (S), Helioprojective Latitude]', 'multi-stack FAILED TypeError object of type SpectrogramCube has no len()'. git rev-list HEAD..main = 80. Running `grep -n 'gwcs_raster\|RasterCollection' IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md` finds nothing. `gh pr list -R LM-SAL/irispy --state open` returns []. #116/#119/#120/#121/#136/#150 are CLOSED and #159 MERGED 2026-08-05. Glue issue #2574 is OPEN. glue origin/main component.py:248,270 and coordinate_helpers.py:189 use world_n_dim-1-axis. The loader code is glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py:104-118.
### wp1-links-2 — D1's 186/187 comes from a method the plan forbids, not from link_hpc
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** D1's 186/187 came from LinkSame, plus a lambda time ComponentLink, plus an all-ones correlation matrix. The plan forbids the all-ones matrix at 653-655. Its no-lambda rule (656-657) is written as a WP4 contract, not a global ban. link_hpc alone raises IncompatibleAttribute for SJI ROI to raster, and the plan deliberately keeps the SJI world-time link out of scope (715; WP4 823-826). So D1 cites a reach from a path that no work package delivers.
- **Recommendation:** Restate D1's evidence as what link_hpc actually gives: per-frame raster->SJI and world-coordinate subsets. Either assign a serializable time link (a precomputed SJI-seconds component plus an identity link) to WP1 or WP4, or drop the 186/187 figure.
- **Evidence:** viewer-composition.txt:126,141,161 describe the all-ones configuration. wp1 test_linking.py:99-104 expects IncompatibleAttribute. d1_reach.py (core-ape14 + WP1) prints 'link_hpc only IncompatibleAttribute' and 'link_hpc + time link steps reached 186 / 187'. Plan 653-657 and 715.
### wp1-links-3 — D1's 14/187 reach is an artefact of a broken 0.8.1 test header
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** irispy 0.8.1's bundled SJI header has CDELT 0.16635, 10x too fine: 37 px cover about 6 arcsec against FOVX 61.2. irispy #171 (e80c51f, an ancestor of v0.9.0 but not of v0.8.1) repaired it to 1.6635. On the #2595 head the pixel-link reach is 12/187 on 0.8.1 and 124/187 on 0.9.0, not 14/187, and SJI (25,25) maps to step 40 rather than 3. The 42.22 arcsec drift holds on both versions.
- **Recommendation:** Cite only the drift, or re-measure reach on the 0.9.0 fixtures or real full-resolution data. Label the audit's geometric numbers as specific to the 0.8.1 fixture.
- **Evidence:** Header probe: 0.8.1 CDELT1 0.16635, NAXIS1 37, FOVX 61.2168; 0.9.0 CDELT1 1.6635. 'git merge-base --is-ancestor e80c51f v0.9.0' succeeds, and the e80c51f message states CDELTn was 10x too small. d1_reach.py prints 'steps reached 12 / 187' on 0.8.1 and '124 / 187' on 0.9.0, with 'diff 42.22 arcsec' on both. pt.py maps SJI(25,25) to (slit 24.46, step 2.98) on 0.8.1 and (24.0, 40.01) on 0.9.0. Sources: plan 632 and review_20260923/README.md:27-28.
### wp1-links-4 — irispy 0.9.0 is released and breaks the WP1 and audit fixture lookups
*plan_stale, confirmed*

- **Verified:** irispy-lmsal 0.9.0 was released on 2026-09-10 and renames the bundled fixtures to *_test.fits. The plan never mentions 0.9.0; line 212 only notes that main's fixtures handle _test.fits names. The WP1 `_real` helper and the audit's `real()` match exact names, so on 0.9.0 the WP1 linking tests error out while main passes. I checked the audit test by code inspection only and did not run it on 0.9.0.
- **Recommendation:** Use conftest.find_irispy_test_file in the WP1 and audit tests, and add irispy 0.9.0 to the validated baseline.
- **Evidence:** PyPI JSON shows 0.9.0 uploaded 2026-09-10T21:35:54, and 'gh release list' lists v0.9.0 as Latest. pyproject.toml:22 has irispy-lmsal>=0.8.1. run_checks with the v0.9.0 source: WP1 test_linking gives '1 failed, 1 skipped, 9 errors' (StopIteration at test_linking.py:36), while main glue_solar/tests gives '33 passed, 2 skipped'. review_20260923/test_wcs_capabilities.py:13-14 uses the same exact-name match.
### wp1-links-5 — WP1's copy of test_importer.py is stale and fails 2 tests
*prototype_test_failure, confirmed*

- **Verified:** WP1's test_importer.py (mtime 2026-09-02) reverts main's #50/#52 test changes: it drops find_irispy_test_file and its test, and expects 2 main components where the WP1 code now adds a Time component. It also carries the genuine WP1 hunks for the label, Angstrom units and a unit-generic stack round trip. Running the whole WP1 test directory fails 2 tests. The recorded command runs only test_linking and test_plugin, so the failure is hidden. Plan 699-700 does say to port the delta rather than copy the package, which limits the damage.
- **Recommendation:** Port only the three genuine test hunks onto main's test_importer.py, and refresh or remove the stale copy. Run the whole WP1 test directory in the recorded check.
- **Evidence:** 'diff -u glue_solar/tests/test_importer.py wp1/.../test_importer.py' shows these hunks. run_checks on wp1/glue_solar/tests gives '2 failed, 39 passed, 3 skipped', with 'too many values to unpack (expected 2, got 3)' and 'assert 3 == 2'. IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/README.md:113-115 runs only test_linking.py and test_plugin.py.
### wp1-links-6 — D3 and canonical names skip glue-solar's own sunpy Map loader
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** glue-solar's own sunpy Map paths assign the raw map WCS, so these datasets show Hplt/Hpln in degrees, which conflicts with settled decision D3. The qglue parser and data factory do this at maps.py:27, and the directory importer does it separately at loaders/maps.py:68-70, which the finding did not cite. Plan 707-708 deliberately scopes these maps out. Neither the WP1 nor the audit prototype tests run wcs_autolink on a sunpy map, although #2595 already links raster and SJI to one correctly on main and on WP1.
- **Recommendation:** Either wrap sunpy maps in `_GlueWCS` in maps.py so they meet D3 and link_hpc covers them, or scope D3 to IRIS datasets explicitly. Add the sunpy-map autolink regression that the brief promises.
- **Evidence:** sunpy_pair.py prints "sunpy map world names ['Hplt', 'Hpln'] ['deg', 'deg']", 1 WCSLink for SJI and for raster, and 'link out [[61.09,61.56,62.15],[42.39,42.52,42.59]]' equal to the expected values on both main and WP1. wp1 test_linking.py:128-136 tests only link_hpc with the AIA map, and test_wcs_capabilities.py has no sunpy import.
### wp2-wp6-science-3 — WP6 ignores the source mask: missing spectra get finite fits and missing samples bias the fits, although parallel_fit_dask supports mask=
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** WP6 clips at zero and applies nan_to_num (wp6_fitting.py:61). Missing samples (-200, flagged in the loader mask) therefore become zeros, and fully missing spectra get finite fits stuck at the seed centroid and width. Partly missing samples bias amplitude and sigma, while parallel_fit_dask's mask= (applied as zero weights) recovers the truth. I downgraded severity for three reasons: the plan already documents the limitation (plan:894-899, README:35), WP6 is not a milestone prerequisite, and plan:495 separately lists masks/quality as WP2/WP6 scope, which is an internal inconsistency.
- **Recommendation:** In v1, build mask = loader mask | ~isfinite, zero only those samples and pass mask= to parallel_fit_dask. Do not clip negative values. NaN out spectra that are fully masked. Add a noisy, zero-background synthetic test that would fail with clipping.
- **Evidence:** fit_real.py (2018 Si IV 1403, every 4th step, 43840 spectra) printed 'fully-missing with finite fit 1687/1687'; the fully-missing fits had amp ~3.7e-28, mu 1402.77 (the seed), sd 0.05. fit_test.py printed 24/24 (2014 Si IV) and 561/561 (sns Mg II), with 848 and 6919 partly missing spectra. synth.py printed 'miss->0 amp 3.655 sd 0.0069', 'clip0 bg 1.168 sd 0.0528' and 'mask= bg -0.102 amp 10.916 mu 1402.7981 sd 0.0609'. The mask handling is in .venv astropy/modeling/_fitting_parallel.py:505-510.
### wp2-wp6-science-4 — WP6 publishes invalid fits (negative sigma or amplitude) with no quality filtering
*prototype_bug, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The numbers reproduce: NaN fraction 0.000, sigma<0 6.0%, amp<0 11.2%. There is no quality filter, and the NaN fraction does not measure fit failure. However, negative sigma is mostly the Gaussian sign degeneracy rather than a failed fit: 62% of the sigma<0 fits have amp>0, median |sigma| 0.081 Å and centroids within ±0.5 Å of rest. Those fits should be published as |sigma|, not NaN'd wholesale. Gallery 01 sets negative sigma to NaN 'for the purpose of the color scale', not as a quality filter, and plan:892-893 already disclaims physical certification.
- **Recommendation:** Before publishing, NaN out fits with sigma <= 0, emission amplitude <= 0, a centroid outside the fit window or non-converged fit_info. Alternatively use a bounded fitter (TRFLSQFitter). Report the rejected fraction to the user.
- **Evidence:** fit_real.py printed 'NaN 0.000; sigma<0 0.060; amp<0 0.112' (43840 spectra) and, on a 10960-spectrum subset, 'sigma<0 fits: amp>0 frac 0.621, median |sigma| 0.081 A ..., centroid within +-0.5A of 1402.77 frac 0.985'. git show v0.8.1:examples/analysis/01_spectral_fitting.py:235-236 shows the colour-scale comment. Gaussian1D stddev bounds are (1.18e-38, None), yet LMLSQFitter results still came back negative.
### wp2-wp6-science-5 — CRISPEX Doppler image (wing difference) is missing from the plan and should come before WP6
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** CRISPEX computes an in-program Doppler image: the difference between the image at the mirrored spectral position lp_dop = 2*lc - lp and the image at the current position. The plan has no Doppler image or Dopplergram product; its nearest item is the deferred 'red-blue asymmetry' moments follow-up (plan:1022), which the finding did not mention. CRISPEX has no moment or Gaussian fitting. Glue's elementwise arithmetic cannot take slices along the wavelength axis, so this is a genuine gap.
- **Recommendation:** Add a Doppler-image product (±Δv around the chosen line rest) to the first milestone, either as a WP5 blink companion or as a small layer_action. It is cheaper than WP6, robust for optically thick lines, and matches CRISPEX directly. Keep WP6 last.
- **Evidence:** git clone --depth 1 github.com/grviss/crispex (c0aa71e): crispex.pro:21000-21002 defines lp_dop, 21054-21059 computes dopslice, and 339 is the v1.6 changelog entry for 'in-program Doppler images'. grep -i -E 'gaussfit|mpfit|moment' crispex.pro found nothing. grep -i 'dopplergram|doppler image' IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md found nothing. The ITN 26 table of contents lists '7.2. Mg II Dopplergrams', and irispy v0.8.1 gallery 02:160 computes doppler = I(-50 km/s) - I(+50 km/s).
### wp2-wp6-science-6 — Plan never addresses optically thick lines (Mg II h/k, C II) for moments or Gaussian fits
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The plan never mentions optically thick lines, central reversals or proxy interpretation. The prototype labels its double Gaussian 'Mg II k2v/k2r' and offers moments and fits for any window. irispy gallery 07 itself calls the double Gaussian 'a simple fitted proxy ... rather than a full radiative-transfer inversion'. The fix is limited to documentation and labelling, so it does not block the quicklook goal.
- **Recommendation:** State in the briefs and user guide which products are physically interpretable (Si IV, O IV, Fe XII, Fe XXI, O I) and which are proxies (Mg II, C II). Do not label Mg II or C II centroids as 'velocity'. Narrow WP6 v1 to single Gaussians on thin lines. Defer the Mg II double Gaussian, or point users to red-blue asymmetry or k2/k3 feature products.
- **Evidence:** grep -n -i -E 'optically|thick|reversal|proxy' IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md returned nothing. wp6_fitting.py:132. git show v0.8.1:examples/analysis/07_mg_ii_two_gaussian_fitting.py:120-127. ITN 38 (https://iris.lmsal.com/itn38/analysis_lines_iris.html) says 'the optically thin lines are mostly observed to have a Gaussian shape'.
### wp2-wp6-science-10 — WP2 moments maps cannot be saved, even with the WP3 prototype
*plan_inconsistent, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The WP2 moments dataset carries irispy's rebuilt 2-axis -TAB WCS (HPLT-TAB, HPLN-TAB). wp3_impl._tab_record indexes pixel_shape[2], so serialising a collection that contains moments maps raises IndexError and the whole save fails. WP3's brief tests only WP6 map round trips (plan:771-772). The WP2 brief says nothing about sessions.
- **Recommendation:** Either build the moments coords like WP6 (_GlueWCS(SlicedLowLevelWCS(raw_wcs, wavelength index 0))) instead of irispy's rebuilt WCS, or generalize _tab_record. Add a WP2 round-trip test next to the WP6 one.
- **Evidence:** sess.py printed 'moments coords _GlueWCS WCS ['HPLT-TAB', 'HPLN-TAB'] pixel_n_dim 2' and 'SERIALIZE FAILED IndexError tuple index out of range at wp3_impl.py 43 _tab_record'. IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/wp3_impl.py:41-43 loops 'for axis in (2, 3): ... wcs.pixel_shape[axis - 1]'.
### wp2-wp6-science-17 — irispy 0.9.0 was released before the plan update but was not validated
*plan_stale, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** irispy v0.9.0 (2026-09-10) is not recorded in the plan, and its moments/meta API change is compatible. The finding misses that the WP2/WP6 prototype tests break on 0.9.0. The bundled test files were renamed *_test.fits, and the prototypes match exact names; glue-solar main's conftest already handles the suffix. The files were also re-decimated to full windows, which invalidates plan:893 ('bundled windows omit line cores') and hard-coded numbers in the tests.
- **Recommendation:** Record 0.9.0 as the current release and re-run the WP2/WP6 checks against it before porting.
- **Evidence:** gh release list -R LM-SAL/irispy printed 'v0.9.0 Latest 2026-09-10T21:24:11Z'. git diff v0.8.1 v0.9.0 -- irispy/utils/moments.py irispy/meta.py. I ran the recorded command against a git-archive of v0.9.0: '3 failed, 11 passed, 3 errors' (StopIteration). After renaming the fixtures back: '2 failed, 15 passed', with 'assert 6545 == 5354' and 'assert 1405.754... < 1400'. glue_solar/conftest.py find_irispy_test_file ('Match both 0.8.1 filenames and irispy's later explicit _test suffix').
### wp3-wp5-3 — WP3 cannot save WP2 moment maps (-TAB axes are hard-coded)
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** WP2 moment maps carry a 2D HPLT-TAB/HPLN-TAB astropy WCS, from irispy make_spatial_template's cube.wcs.dropaxis. wp3_impl._tab_record hard-codes axes (2, 3), so saving any session that contains a moments map raises IndexError. The WP3 brief (plan:772) lists only WP6 map round trips. Severity is medium rather than high because WP2 is a 'later' extension and the fix is local.
- **Recommendation:** Take the -TAB axes and columns from the PSi_0/PSi_1/PSi_2 keys instead of assuming axes 2/3, or have WP2 rewrap as _GlueWCS(SlicedLowLevelWCS(raw, slices)) like WP6. Add a WP2 map round trip to the WP3 acceptance list.
- **Evidence:** moments_save.py (wp1 + wp3_impl + chk_wp2) printed 'moments inner WCS ['HPLT-TAB', 'HPLN-TAB'] (109, 8)'. The raster saved OK, but the moments save failed with 'wp3_impl.py, line 43, in _tab_record ... IndexError: tuple index out of range'. irispy/utils/_spectral.py:61-62 uses dropaxis.
### wp3-wp5-4 — A restored session loses SGMeta, so the rest wavelength and window properties disappear
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Glue's Data loader always builds a new Data and calls meta.update() with the filtered meta (glue/core/state.py _load_data_5), so even LoadLog-backed IRIS data come back with a plain OrderedDict. SGMeta properties (rest_wavelength, spectral_window) are lost, and km/s is no longer offered after reopening. Combined with wp3-wp5-2, a saved km/s Profile viewer could not restore anyway.
- **Recommendation:** At load time, have the loader write plain meta values (rest_wavelength in Angstrom, window name) and make WP5 read those, or add a scoped SGMeta saver. Add restored-session velocity to the WP3/WP5 acceptance tests.
- **Evidence:** meta_rt.py printed 'LoadLog True orig meta SGMeta 1402.77 Angstrom Si IV 1403', then 'restored meta OrderedDict None False n keys 398', then 'km/s offered orig: True restored: False'. The TWAVE1..n keys survive but _iwin does not.
### wp3-wp5-6 — The WP5 unit converter replaces glue's default for all datasets and narrows their unit choices
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** wp5_units.py:36-40 narrows the choices for every length or angle component of every dataset once it is installed as the global 'default' converter (line 51). The plan's text (846) does not require this narrowing, and it is not needed for the arcsec/Angstrom decision.
- **Recommendation:** Add km/s only for spectral components of datasets with a known rest wavelength, and otherwise fall through to super(). Drop the global narrowing, which also means less code.
- **Evidence:** A probe printed 'stock pc n 131 True True | rad has mas True', then after importing wp5_units: "wp5 pc ['Angstrom', 'nm'] | wp5 rad ['arcsec', 'arcmin', 'deg']".
### wp3-wp5-7 — WP3 acceptance leaves out viewers, subsets, links and WP4 coordination
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The WP3 checklist (plan:757-775) is Data-level only, and wp3chk_test_sessions.py serializes bare DataCollections. WP4 sync is installed per app by wrapping new_data_viewer (wp4_common.py:92-148), with nothing re-attaching it after restore. The plan does acknowledge the need elsewhere: line 494 says 'validate full IRIS application sessions', line 749 admits the tests 'do not ... prove a full coordinated quicklook session', and WP4's test item (plan:861) includes 'serializable links'. What is missing is an explicit application-level acceptance item plus a coordination re-attach step.
- **Recommendation:** Add application-level acceptance: image/profile/whisker viewer states, point/ROI subsets, WCS + link_hpc + WP4 identity links, and coordination re-attached after restore. Make each of WP2/WP4/WP5/WP6 include its own save/reopen test.
- **Evidence:** Plan lines 494, 749, 767-775 and 861, read directly. wp3chk_test_sessions.py:32-35 has _round_trip on a DataCollection. wp4_common.py:92-148 has sync_slices/_install_slice_sync, which only wraps app.new_data_viewer. The reviewer's link_session.py result on core-ape14 was not re-run.
### wp3-wp5-11 — CRISPEX's live Doppler (red-minus-blue wing) image is not in the plan
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** CRISPEX computes a live Doppler image: the image at the mirror position lp_dop = 2*lc - lp minus the current image, shown in its own window with its own scaling. The plan, the prototypes and the archive never mention it; they have only offline moments and a deferred red-blue asymmetry item (plan:1022). The plan should either add it or explicitly defer it.
- **Recommendation:** Decide explicitly: add it to WP5 (for example a derived Δλ-indexed wing-difference cube built from the chosen rest wavelength, viewed with ordinary sliders) or list it as deferred or a non-goal.
- **Evidence:** crispex.pro:20999-21001 (lp_dop = 2*lc - lp), :21054-21060 (dopslice difference), :3331 ('Doppler image' window) and :339 ('in-program Doppler images'). grep -i 'doppler image|dopplergram|wing difference' over the plan and IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/archive, review_20260923 and README gives no hits.
### wp4-quicklook-4 — WP4-added components are materialized in sessions (~8.5 bytes per data element)
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The 'Nearest ...' and 'Slit x' components that WP4 adds have no load log, so glue serializes them as full arrays. With WP3 file-referencing loaders, a session for the sns SJI plus one window grows from 67,608 to 2,695,396 bytes (time links alone: 1,716,144). This conflicts with the WP3 small-session target (plan:771-772); the plan sets no session-size check for WP4.
- **Recommendation:** Persist only the 1-D per-frame index/offset arrays, or recompute them on restore from the Time components. Add this to WP3/WP4 acceptance as a session-size check with time sync active.
- **Evidence:** Released glue/core/state.py:1094-1101 writes data=context.do(component.data) when there is no _load_log. Verifier test_v_session.py via runner (wp1:protos, wp3_impl.install_browser_factories): 'base bytes 67608', 'time bytes 1716144', 'slit bytes 1046860', 'both bytes 2695396', elements 308680. A run of wp4_chk_join.py printed 'session round trip without JoinLink: 10611 KB'.
### wp4-quicklook-7 — quicklook's own layout gets no time coordination for 3D rasters and shows a whole-cube or edge-pixel spectrum
*prototype_not_useful, confirmed*

- **Verified:** quicklook opens only SJI, map (step × slit) and Profile viewers. It has no spectrogram panel and applies no slit or point subset. The profile is an unselected whole-cube Mean on released core, or a Slice fixed at (0,0,0) on #2601 that does not follow the SJI. Changing the SJI frame changes nothing in this layout. The prototype follows the brief (plan:803-806), but the brief itself conflicts with the four-panel criterion and 'a whole-cube mean is not a selected-location spectrum' (plan:424-429).
- **Recommendation:** Make the preset the four criterion panels: map, spectrogram, SJI with slit and point overlay, and point-subset or Slice spectrum. Drive map and profile fixed indices from the selected point and master time. Label the profile's aggregation, and never default to a whole-cube mean.
- **Evidence:** test_v_ql.py::test_quicklook. Released core: 'viewers [ImageViewer, ImageViewer, ProfileViewer]', 'profile function mean slices None subsets 0', 'after SJI frame 30: map slices (0, 0, 14) ... expected step 91'. core-combined + qt-profile: 'profile function slice slices (0, 0, 0)' before and after. Code: wp4_common.py:111-112 and 123-124, 169-200.
### wp4-quicklook-8 — CRISPEX raster-timing marker and raster footprint overlays are not in the plan
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** CRISPEX draws raster footprint overlays (every slit position on the SJI via pix_main2sji) and raster timing markers (the step nearest the master time, on main, reference and SJI). The plan has no criterion for a map-side current-step marker or an SJI raster footprint. However, the SJI-side timing marker is effectively the planned SJI slit overlay (plan:426, WP4 title at 779). Plan:410 also mentions 'raster/SJI overlays' when describing the CRISPEX exercises, so the grep result given is incomplete.
- **Recommendation:** Add to WP4: a current-step marker on the map and SJI for the matched exposure, and an optional raster footprint on the SJI. Reuse the WCS projection needed for the slit overlay.
- **Evidence:** crispex.pro:7181-7200 (CRISPEX_DRAW_RASTER_OVERLAYS looping over nx with pix_main2sji) and 7202-7267 (CRISPEX_DRAW_RASTER_TIMING_OVERLAYS using the master_time CASE). grep of the plan for timing/footprint/outline/overlay: 384, 426, 496, 929-936 (WP7 only), plus 410.
### wp4-quicklook-9 — Wrapping app.new_data_viewer misses session-restored viewers; the repo already has a per-viewer hook pattern
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** No viewer-creation hub message exists in core or Qt (local, origin or upstream main). Session restore builds viewers with cls(session, state=...) and calls add_widget directly, so a wrapped app.new_data_viewer never sees restored viewers; a restored app also lacks the private wrapper. Registered viewer tools are instantiated in initialize_toolbar via the ToolbarInitializer metaclass for every construction, including restore. That is the pattern FrameTimeTool already uses on main. The plan (820-823) acknowledges the wrapping but not the session-restore gap.
- **Recommendation:** Implement sync participation as a registered Image/Profile viewer tool (the solar:frame_time pattern) that registers with one controller per data collection and unregisters in close(). Remove the method wrapping and the private app attribute.
- **Evidence:** glue_qt/app/application.py:1454-1470 (context.object(v); self.add_widget); glue_qt/viewers/common/data_viewer.py:18-30 and 161-184; released glue/viewers/common/viewer.py:397-402. The message class list in glue/core/message.py has no viewer message. 'git grep -i ViewerCreated|viewer_created|ViewerAdded' returns 0 hits on glue and glue-qt main, origin/main and upstream/main. main:glue_solar/__init__.py appends FrameTimeTool.tool_id to ImageViewer.tools.
### wp4-quicklook-10 — sync_slices raises TypeError when Profile Collapse sets an AggregateSlice
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** push() runs int(s) on every slice, and Profile Collapse writes AggregateSlice into ImageViewerState.slices, so sync raises TypeError. The map preset escapes this, because its displayed step axis triggers the early return. SJI and spectrogram viewers do raise. main's FrameTimeTool already handles this case with getattr(s, 'slice', s). (Collapse is at plan:495, not 494.)
- **Recommendation:** Treat AggregateSlice as its .center or skip it, and add a regression test that collapses while sync is active.
- **Evidence:** test_v_ql.py::test_aggregate on both released and #2601 cores: 'AggregateSlice -> TypeError: int() argument must be ... not 'AggregateSlice''. glue_qt/viewers/profile/profile_tools.py:324-332 writes the AggregateSlice; released glue/viewers/image/state.py:51; main:glue_solar/tools.py _refresh; wp4_common.py:113.
### wp4-quicklook-11 — link_time fails for V34 (flipped) rasters because step times descend
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** For V34 observations (STEPS_AV < -0.01), irispy reverses times and data along the step axis. The raster Time component therefore descends, nearest() rejects the unsorted reference, and link_time (and so quicklook) raises ValueError. No V34 fixture exists, so this was reproduced with synthetic descending times.
- **Recommendation:** Argsort the reference, match against the sorted times, and map the result back to the original indices. Test with descending step times.
- **Evidence:** irispy 0.8.1 io/spectrograph.py:158 (v34 = STEPS_AV < -0.01), 258-260 (times = t_obs[::-1]; np.flip data), 273 (extra_coords time). WP1 _frame_times reads that extra coordinate (wp1 iris.py:179-187). test_v_ql.py::test_v34: 'V34 -> ValueError times must be finite and reference times sorted'. wp4_common.py:43-44 and 61-63.
### wp4-quicklook-12 — 'Raster-map ROIs should still reach SJI' passes with physically wrong selections
*plan_incorrect, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Reproduced for a map ROI (steps 40-60, slit 10-20) on the sns pair. Without #2595 it selects full-width SJI stripes: 37/37 columns, frames 14-19 with time links and 10-24 without. With #2595 it selects 0 px. The audit's claim that 'spatial ROIs propagate in both directions' (plan:490, 561) was tested only with a whole-image ROI. The finding's slit position x≈21-23 comes from the fixture's unscaled-CDELT WCS; SLTPX1IX/10 puts it at about 18.7. Why #2595 gives 0 px was not verified.
- **Recommendation:** Define the expected selection per observation type: spatial footprint at the matched frame for rasters, time-gated slit strip for sit-and-stare. Assert on columns and frames rather than 'non-empty'.
- **Evidence:** test_v_roi.py via runner. Released: 'wcs=False time=True: 2220/91760 px frames [14..19] cols 37/37 rows 11..21'; 'time=False: 2410 ... frames [10..24] cols 37/37'. core-combined: 'wcs=True time=False/True: 0/91760 px'. review_20260923/test_wcs_capabilities.py:63-69 uses RectangularROI(-1, shape+1, -1, shape+1). A wp4_chk_join.py run printed 'raster MAP ROI ... -> SJI: 2220/91760 px'.
### wp4-quicklook-13 — Point follow/hold mapping is left as 'decide'; nothing specifies which indices a selected point sets
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The plan states the requirement at plan:430-435, 486, 587-590, 810-812 and 816-820 but gives no per-panel mapping of which fixed indices, and which time master, a selected point sets for 3D raster, sit-and-stare and 4D stack. It never says whether selecting a step moves the time master. wp4_common has no subset or Pixel coordination, which README:32 and the audit README:44-45 admit.
- **Recommendation:** Add a per-panel mapping table to the WP4 brief (source panel, selected point, target fixed indices, time effect) for 3D raster, sit-and-stare and 4D stack. Pick one default policy (detector pixel is simplest with frame-0 WCS) and add its tests.
- **Evidence:** grep of wp4_common.py for subset/Pixel finds only slit_subset_state (lines 31-33) and no SubsetUpdateMessage or PixelSubsetState. IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/README.md:32 ('no cursor follow/lock, profile coordination'). review_20260923/README.md:44-45. Plan lines above read directly.
### wp7-wp8-2 — WP8: clicking Load during a rescan loads from the replaced observation list
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Reproduced. `ok` stays enabled while a rescan runs, because set_directory never disables it (wp8_proto.py:31-46). finalize() calls processEvents per pick (iris.py:300-303), which delivers the queued _scanned. That call replaces self.observations and repopulates while the picks still hold the old indices. Stopping the worker at the start of finalize would not fix this, because _stop_scan does not bump _scan_id and so the partial result is still delivered. Disabling Load while scanning is the robust fix.
- **Recommendation:** Add to the brief: disable `ok` while scanning, or have finalize() first stop the scan and bump _scan_id. Add a test that clicks Load while a slow rescan is running.
- **Evidence:** Scratch test test_v_wp8.py::test_f2_load_during_rescan. Before the rescan, the pick was (3,'raster','C II 1336') for obsid 3400109360 with 2 raster files. The rescan was gated with threading.Event, then worker.wait() and ok.click(). Output: 'calls [([], ['C II 1336'])] populate [True, True]', 1 passed. git status clean.
### wp7-wp8-5 — WP7 GOES flux ignores XRS quality flags
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Confirmed. The sunpy 8.0.0 XRS reader returns xrsa_quality and xrsb_quality columns without masking the flux. The prototype copies only xrsa and xrsb (context_chk.py:53-54), and plan 919-923 says nothing about quality. sunpy's own GOES example filters out rows where either flag is non-zero. A quicklook can therefore plot flagged samples, and the brief should say to mask them.
- **Recommendation:** Set flagged samples to NaN, or keep the quality components and filter them, and state this in the brief. Add a mocked test with a flagged row.
- **Evidence:** .venv sunpy/timeseries/sources/goes.py:232-235 (flags read), 267 (DataFrame with quality columns) and 272-273 (dimensionless units). No masking was found by grep. WebFetch of https://docs.sunpy.org/en/stable/generated/gallery/time_series/goes_xrs_example.html quotes: df = df[(df["xrsa_quality"] == 0) & (df["xrsb_quality"] == 0)].
### wp7-wp8-9 — WP7 SDO action overlaps the local co-aligned cutouts and blocks the GUI during a ~65 MiB download
*prototype_not_useful, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The size and blocking claims are confirmed. VSO returns one 64.6 MiB JSOC file, which downloads synchronously under a wait cursor (context_chk.py:166-168). The plan does, however, knowingly defer threading and says to document the wait (937-938). The stronger point is that the IDL counterpart is a 200 px full-disk LMSAL SunInTime thumbnail, differentially rotated, with the IRIS FOV box. The audit lists 'limb/full-disk context' as missing. The plan's crop of FOV plus 100 arcsec (928-930) does not provide that full-disk pointing context, and it largely overlaps the per-exposure LMSAL cutouts the browser already loads.
- **Recommendation:** State when the VSO fetch is preferable to local co-aligned cutouts. Consider a configurable or larger pointing-context margin. Reuse WP8's Worker pattern for the download rather than deferring threading.
- **Evidence:** Live Fido.search(near=start) returned 1 row, ['JSOC'], 64.64844 MiB. archive/2026-09-05/IRIS_IDL_GLUE_CAPABILITY_AUDIT.md:89 and :229 ('No live SDO retrieval, limb/full-disk context...'). WebFetch of sdo_icon__define.pro: 'Retrieves and stores small t SDO images', size=200, url sdowww.lmsal.com/sdomedia/SunInTime, drot_map. WebFetch of xpointing_image.pro: draws the FOV box on the supplied image, with no crop.
### plan-integrity-2 — Documented test runs overwrite the user's real macOS QSettings
*other, confirmed*

- **Verified:** run_checks.py redirects CFG_DIR/XDG/MPL/sunpy but not QSettings. QtIRISImporter(tmp_path) calls set_directory, which writes QSettings('glue-solar','glue-solar') iris.last_dir to the real macOS defaults domain, and isolating HOME does not stop it. This breaks plan L337-339 ('do not silently edit ... QSettings'). L1076-1078 is literally true only for Glue's own config. The harm is limited to a last-directory preference.
- **Recommendation:** Make the runner (or glue_solar/conftest.py) redirect QSettings, e.g. patch iris.QSettings to an IniFormat file under the temp root. Add this to the plan's validation notes.
- **Evidence:** run_checks.py:7-24 (no QSettings handling); glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py:204-205,216-220; glue_solar/tests/test_importer.py:18,57,216,242 construct QtIRISImporter(dir). My own run 'env HOME=<scratch>/home2 ... run_checks.py <irispy090> glue_solar' then 'defaults read com.glue-solar.glue-solar' -> iris.last_dir='.../pytest-445/test_reader_failure_stays_in_d0' (test_importer.py:227). Before that run it already held pytest-422/pooch0 and then pytest-437 paths from other runs. git status --short stayed clean.
### plan-integrity-6 — The sequence treats world links as optional, contradicting D1 (keep link_hpc permanently)
*plan_inconsistent, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The wording is weaker than D1, and a test is missing. L570 'Retain the separate world-coordinate helper where needed' and L585 'before choosing any extra world links' can be read as making link_hpc conditional. WP1 L706-708 and WP0 L307-309 do mandate link_hpc with browser invocation, so the plan does not actually make it optional. The real gap: L571-573 requires testing the combined pixel+world+nearest-index link graph, but no WP1/WP4 checkbox includes it, and the WCS probes do not install world/time links.
- **Recommendation:** Reword step 2 to 'add link_hpc alongside the WCS links (D1)'. Add a WP1 checkbox that tests APE-14 WCS links plus link_hpc (plus WP4 time links) together, with no unintended path selection.
- **Evidence:** IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md L307-309, L570-574, L583-585, L629-633, L706-719 (grep of WP1 section for 'combined|graph' -> no hit), L824-828; IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/review_20260923/README.md L51-54 'WP1 does not install additional world/time links during these checks'.
### plan-integrity-7 — Several keyboard-table requirements have no owning checkbox
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** No WP4 or WP5 checkbox owns the band/mean/sum/single-sample controls (L488) or the persistent subset combination rule (L517-519). WP4 L804 only says 'Use mean profiles on the released baseline'. The UTC coordinate adapter is owned as a decision by WP4 L816-818 ('Decide whether physical UTC axes are needed') and is optional per L594-595, so that part is acceptable. What is missing is an owner if the decision is yes.
- **Recommendation:** Assign each of these to a WP checkbox with a done condition, or move them explicitly to deferred.
- **Evidence:** grep -i 'band|sum|single-sample|combination' over plan L779-866 -> no hits; only 'UTC' at L818. Plan L487-488, L517-519, L594-595, L803-807, L816-818.
### plan-integrity-8 — The end-to-end and performance acceptance criterion has no owner or thresholds
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The end-to-end and performance criterion (L452-456) has no owning WP, queue row, named reference dataset or latency/memory budget. L1050 defers it as 'First-milestone performance evidence'. The criterion asks to 'Record' sizes, latency and memory, which is closable without thresholds, so 'cannot be closed objectively' is overstated. For a robust quicklook on large IRIS data, the missing owner and reference dataset are real gaps.
- **Recommendation:** Add an owned acceptance task (e.g. WP4 or WP0), name the reference observation(s), and state the pass/fail latency and memory budget.
- **Evidence:** IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md L452-456 ('Record input sizes, interaction latency and memory'), L458-463 (primary delivery names WP1+WP4+WP5, not this task), queue L678-689 (no acceptance/perf row), L1050.
### archive-trace-2 — Reinstalling coordination after session restore is not tracked
*dropped_from_archive, confirmed*

- **Verified:** No WP3 or WP4 task says how selected-point, slice-sync or time-matching coordination comes back after a session is restored. The first milestone (452-454) and step 5 (596-599) still require a reopened session. The prototype hooks `app.new_data_viewer` and stores its hook on the app instance, but restore builds a new application and adds viewers through add_widget, so nothing is reinstalled.
- **Recommendation:** Add a WP3/WP4 acceptance check: after restoring a saved quicklook session, the Pixel point, time matching and slice sync work again without rerunning the browser. Also name the mechanism (session hook, or saved state that rebuilds the controller).
- **Evidence:** glue-qt main glue_qt/app/application.py:1453-1470: classmethod __setgluestate__ calls self.add_widget(viewer, tab=i, hold_position=True). wp4_common.py:92-100 stores the hook as app._iris_hook_viewer. Plan 494, 820-823 and the WP3 tasks 757-777 have no reinstall task. Archive 3152 records the pitfall.
### archive-trace-3 — Rest wavelength (and the km/s unit) is lost after session restore
*dropped_from_archive, confirmed*

- **Verified:** After a session round trip, a real raster's meta becomes a plain OrderedDict and WP5's helper returns None, so km/s is no longer offered. The restored meta still holds TWAVE1..n, but neither the helper nor the plan uses them. The current plan copies a rest value only for stacks (848-849), and WP3 has no task to write `meta['rest_wavelength']` for scans. Archive 3723 had asked for exactly that.
- **Recommendation:** Have the loader write a float `meta['rest_wavelength']` (Angstrom) for every raster window, not just stacks. Add a WP3/WP5 test that km/s is still available after a real-raster session round trip.
- **Evidence:** My runner run (scratch test_vat_rest.py, roots solar+IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES, wp3_impl + wp5_units, 2014 Mg II k 2796 r00000) printed 'REST meta types SGMeta -> OrderedDict', 'REST before 2796.1999511699996 Angstrom after None TWAVE keys after [TWAVE1..4]' and 'REST km/s offered before True after False'. In the venv, state.py:923 builds a new Data(label) and :1060 calls meta.update.
### archive-trace-4 — TWAVE-versus-laboratory rest wavelength caveat was dropped; defaults still use TWAVE
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** When no rest wavelength is given, both WP2 (733, irispy default) and WP5 (846-848, irispy metadata) fall back to SGMeta.rest_wavelength, which is TWAVE. The plan never says that TWAVE is the window's reference wavelength rather than a laboratory value. For Mg II k this shifts the velocity zero point by -16.1 km/s. That undercuts criterion 450-451 ('scientifically appropriate rest wavelength'), and the packaged line list already holds the laboratory value, 2796.35.
- **Recommendation:** Record the TWAVE caveat in WP2/WP5. Label the TWAVE fallback in the UI as 'window reference, not laboratory', or default to the matching laboratory value from the packaged line list. Test the Mg II k 16 km/s case.
- **Evidence:** irispy SGMeta.rest_wavelength returns float(TWAVE{iwin}) (venv irispy meta.py:194-198), and moments.py:100-102 defaults to cube.meta.rest_wavelength. Archive 3440-3446 table: Mg II k TWAVE 2796.20 vs laboratory 2796.35 -> -16.1 km/s; Si IV 1394 -> +4.3 km/s. Archive 3720 says 'Keep the override action; document it'. IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/iris_lines.csv lists 2796.35 for Mg II k. The current plan mentions TWAVE only at 849, 879 and 1112.
### usefulness-1 — First milestone is not thin; a quicklook M0 can ship before WP1, WP3 and WP5
*sequencing_or_priority, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The first milestone does bundle six criteria, including a reopened WP3 session, same-cube blink/Doppler and large-data evidence (plan 424-456), and the minimal sequence puts WP5/WP3 at step 5. The WP4 prototype needs only `link_hpc` and `_frame_times` from WP1, and `_frame_times` already ships on main, so a thinner M0 is feasible. However, the plan already calls its queue 'preferred sequencing, not invented hard dependencies' (676) and says WP2/WP6/WP7 are not prerequisites (458-463). M0 would still need WP1's `link_hpc`, and SJI-click-to-raster needs #2595 (see usefulness-2). This is a scoping recommendation, not a blocker.
- **Recommendation:** Define M0 as: launch entry, observation preset, Pixel point to fixed indices, nearest-exposure SJI/raster sync and slit overlay on released core. Move WP1 Angstrom/high-level coherence, WP3, WP5 blink/Doppler and performance evidence to M1. Mark WP2, WP6, WP7 and WP8 as explicitly outside M0/M1.
- **Evidence:** wp4_common.py:6 imports `_frame_times, link_hpc`. Main glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py:88-97 defines `_frame_times`, but main has no `link_hpc`. `.venv` probe printed 'x_display_unit True' and '1.27.0'. Plan lines 389, 424-456, 458-463, 576-599 and 676-685 checked.
### usefulness-2 — SJI-click to raster spectrum has an unstated hard dependency on unreleased core #2595
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Reproduced: on released core 1.27.0, `link_hpc` LinkSame world links leave the pixel correlation matrix all zero, so an SJI PixelSubsetState cannot reach the raster (IncompatibleAttribute). A direct WCSLink fails with the `has_celestial` AttributeError, and autolink suggests 0 links; with core-combined, the WCSLink builds and autolink suggests 1. Main has no `link_hpc` at all. The dependency is not unstated: the feature table assigns SJI/raster pixel links to 'WP1 + core #2595' (490), and 562-564 says these paths work 'with solar main plus #2595'. What is missing is that dependency, or a released-core fallback, in the milestone criterion (430).
- **Recommendation:** State in the milestone that SJI-point-to-raster needs a #2595 release, or specify a glue-solar interim under D2. The has_celestial error hints at a wrapper-side shim, but that is unverified. Until then, M0 should drive the raster from raster-map clicks and treat SJI clicks as M1.
- **Evidence:** Scratch test_vu_probes.py via run_checks.py. With wp1 on released core: 'LINK_HPC n 2', 'MATRIX [[0,0,0]x3]', 'XY-ERR IncompatibleAttribute', 'SPECTRUM-ERR IncompatibleAttribute', 'DIRECT-ERR AttributeError '_GlueWCS' object has no attribute 'has_celestial'', 'AUTOLINK n 0'. With core-combined: 'DIRECT OK 2 2', 'AUTOLINK n 1', and the matrix still zero with link_hpc only. On main: 'NO link_hpc on this package'. Plan 490 and 560-564 checked.
### usefulness-3 — The WP4 quicklook preset does not show the behaviour it is meant to seed, and has no real-data test
*prototype_not_useful, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Reproduced on the matched 2021 fixture: moving the SJI to frame 61 left the preset raster map at (0,0,14), and only a user-opened spectrogram moved to (186,0,0). The profile function is 'mean' with no subset, which is a whole-cube mean. `quicklook()` never calls `add_slit`/`slit_subset_state`, so no slit overlay is created. The 0-subset-groups metric is the wrong measure, because the plan wants a data-only subset. The raster map is expected not to move, because it displays step as an axis, and the README (32) and plan (783) already say the prototype is image-slider sync only, with no profile coordination. The only pytest of `quicklook` does stub `new_data_viewer` with an empty dataset list; wp4_b_quicklook.py and wp4_f_sync.py are real-data scripts without asserts.
