- **Recommendation:** Treat wp4_common.py as a reference for `nearest`, `link_time` and `observation_key` only. Build M0's preset against real-fixture tests that assert which panel moves on each interaction.
- **Evidence:** Scratch probe output: 'VIEWERS [ImageViewer, ImageViewer, ProfileViewer]', 'PROFILE function mean', 'SUBSET groups 0 data subsets [0, 0]', 'RASTERMAP (0, 0, 14) -> (0, 0, 14)', 'SPECTROGRAM (0, 0, 0) -> (186, 0, 0)'. wp4_common.py:169-200 contains no add_slit call. review_20260905/test_plan_integration.py:22-40 is stubbed. Plan 428-429 and 803-805 differ on whether a mean profile is acceptable.
### usefulness-4 — The preset's panel set is inconsistent, and the whisker ignores the plan's time semantics for stacks
*plan_inconsistent, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The criteria list four panels, including a wavelength-versus-slit spectrogram (424-427) and a time–wavelength view (441-446). WP4 (803-807) and the prototype open only SJI, raster map and Profile. `whisker` hard-codes y = pix[-3], which is step for a 4D stack. That contradicts the plan's Scan × Wavelength recipe for repeated rasters (504-507) and its warning that step is not a time series (444-446). WP4's own text, 'wavelength versus step/time', is ambiguous for stacks.
- **Recommendation:** Add one small table per observation type (single raster, sit-and-stare, multi-scan stack) giving panels, x/y axes and the fixed indices driven by a point. Make it the WP4 definition of done.
- **Evidence:** wp4_common.py:78-89 (`viewer.state.x_att, viewer.state.y_att = pix[-1], pix[-3]`) and 184-198 (three viewers). IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md:424-427, 441-446, 504-507 and 803-807.
### usefulness-6 — WP5 unit override narrows unit choices for every Glue dataset
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** `wp5_units.install()` runs at import and replaces `unit_converter.members['default']` process-wide. For a plain non-IRIS 'm' component, the choices drop from 131 to ['Angstrom', 'nm']. For 'deg' they drop from 104 to ['arcsec', 'arcmin']. Once ported into glue-solar, which loads through the glue.plugins entry point in every session, this would narrow units for all datasets, contrary to 'offer ... only when meaningful' (844-847).
- **Recommendation:** Keep the default-name override, which avoids the settings.cfg trap, but return `super()` results unless the data is IRIS (has OBSID or a rest wavelength). Add a regression test for non-IRIS length and angle units.

### usefulness-12 — Large-dataset requirements are missing; memory and a blocking modal are the real risks
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Confirmed: loaders read with `memmap=False` (main loaders/iris.py:141,147,174) inside the GUI-thread `finalize` loop. Local rasters are 3.17 GB (8 windows × 400×1095) and 4.14 GB (9 windows × 1600×417) as float32. The Mg II k window has 235M points, above ProfileViewer's large_data_size=1e8, which calls `warn(..., default='Cancel')`. The plan names no memory/latency budget and never mentions this modal. I did not reproduce the 6.67 GB peak RSS, the >600 s hang or the latency figures. The browser loads only the ticked windows (`raster_data(obs.rasters, [name])`), so a typical load is about 1-1.5 GB per window rather than the whole file, and the plan does gate the milestone on recording size, latency and memory (454-456).
- **Recommendation:** Add a non-functional section with a memory budget (for example peak RSS no more than 2x the loaded data), an evaluation of `memmap=True`, loading off the GUI thread, and preset handling of the large-data modal. *plan_inconsistent, confirmed*

*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

### followup-1-3 — Link 'order' does not decide the WCSLink versus time-link tie; memory-address set order does
*plan_incorrect, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The technical claim is confirmed. discover_links relaxes to the minimum hop count; at equal cost the first accepted link wins (cost >= depth skips later ones). Links are iterated from a set of object-hashed links, so the winner is not controlled by add order. The kind is wrong, though: the plan never claims order control; only the scratch probe labelled results by order. The proposed rule is too broad: equal-hop alternatives are harmless when they compute the same value, and the rule should forbid equal-hop links that give different values for one target.
- **Recommendation:** Record in the plan that glue gives no ordering guarantee. A correct graph must never have two links of equal hop count that reach the same component ID from one dataset. The regression test should run every permutation plus a loop that perturbs memory allocation.
- **Evidence:** link_manager.py:71-94 (depth/cost loop), :250 (self._links | self._inverse_links), :268-288 (set construction); component_link.py:29 defines no __hash__/__eq__; link_manager.py and component_link.py are cmp-SAME vs 1.27.0. My runs of test_tie: winners with fixed add order and perturbed allocation ['ComponentLink','ComponentLink','identity','identity','identity','identity'] (0.8.1) and ['identity','ComponentLink','ComponentLink','ComponentLink','identity','ComponentLink'] (0.9.0). The set of P orders passing (c) differs per run: prior JSONs {wcs,hpc,time; wcs,time,hpc; hpc,wcs,time; hpc,time,wcs}; mine {hpc,time,wcs} (0.8.1) and {wcs,time,hpc; time,wcs,hpc} (0.9.0). The prior 'run 1' set could not be re-read, because matrix_081.json equals run2.
### followup-1-4 — D1 rationale (14/187 vs 186/187) is not delivered by link_hpc once WCSLinks exist, and the numbers are irispy-0.8.1-specific
*plan_incorrect, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Confirmed: with #2595, SJI ROI reach on the raster is set by WCSLink and is identical with or without link_hpc: 12/187 on 0.8.1 and 124/187 on 0.9.0, with marker (24,3) versus (24,40). D1 is still needed for check (d), which fails with WCSLink alone. Correction on provenance: 14/187 was measured with the fork WCSLink at 016d9cf2 (steps 0-13), not with #2595 (12/187 on 0.8.1). The 186/187 figure came from LinkSame + a time link + an all-ones correlation matrix, which plan:653 now forbids; design E reproduces 186 via the WP1 glue_patches inverse workaround.
- **Recommendation:** Keep D1 unchanged, but restate its purpose. link_hpc supplies exact, time-aware cross-dataset world coordinates and world-component subsets (check d). WCSLink supplies frame-0 pixel paths for ROIs, markers and overlays. Mark 14/187 as an irispy 0.8.1 fixture number (0.9.0 gives 124/187 and marker (slit, step) (24,40) instead of (24,3)), and do not pin either value in tests.
- **Evidence:** test_indep.py mechanism: core-combined 0.8.1 'wcs+hpc ... reach=12/187' and 'wcs only ... reach=12/187'; 0.9.0 '124/187' in both, get_xy=(24,40). My test_d_needs_hpc: 'wcs only: sji[ras lat]==sji lat: False, ras[sji lat]==ras lat: IncompatibleAttribute'; 'wcs+hpc: all True'. My r081.log E rows: lead_hit 186 with a=False, e=False. Provenance: IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/wp9chk/viewer-composition.txt:85, 125-126, 139, 164 and brief-checker-reports.txt:287 ('Not re-verified: fork WCSLink ... 14/187'); WP1 inverse patch at wp1/glue_solar/glue_patches.py:10-48.
### followup-1-5 — Adding the SJI-world-time link that WP1 treats as a missing piece would break the SJI Pixel spectrum and overlays
*plan_incorrect, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Confirmed: adding the D1-archive SJI world-time link (raster Time to SJI world time) on top of WCSLink + link_hpc (design F) breaks (a) and (e) in 6/6 orders on irispy 0.8.1 and 0.9.0. ### followup-2-4 — The WP6 float64 residual cube doubles RAM and embeds hundreds of MB in sessions
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** WP6 casts the cube to float64 and stores cube minus model as a float64 residual component on the source, twice the float32 window. Because that component is not LoadLog-backed, it is embedded in sessions. Almost all of the 640 MB fit session is the residual: the maps account for about 9 MB, and the 'fit products removed' comparison also removed the maps. The 2.96 GB figure for sit-and-stare Mg II k is arithmetic (370,296,000 × 8 B), not a measurement.
- **Recommendation:** WP6: store the residual as float32 in the source's dtype, or make it opt-in or recomputable from the parameter maps. WP3 threshold: fit products add at most 2x the 2-D map bytes to a session unless the user opts into embedding the residual.
- **Evidence:** wp6_fitting.py:61 has np.asarray(cube, dtype=float), :89 returns cube - model, and :123 calls add_component(Component(residual)). s3_fit_session_si.txt shows residual ('float64', 472770560) = 320*548*337*8, 'SESSION with fit products: 639802769 bytes' and 'removed: 53703 bytes'.

*missing_requirement, confirmed*

This affects the sit-and-stare windows and the 99-scan stack, which are above 1e8 elements. The 2018 rasters (5.9e7) are below the threshold. The user can press OK or tick 'Don't show again', and plan line 1109 only says to mock modals in headless tests.
- **Recommendation:** WP4: add IRIS data to the quicklook Profile viewer without the modal, for example by setting large_data_size=None on that viewer, and keep a size notice non-blocking. Run the real-size quicklook test without the monkeypatch.
- **Evidence:** glue viewer.py:200-203 warns with default='Cancel'. glue-qt profile/data_viewer.py:25 sets large_data_size = 1e8, and data_viewer.py:6,123 routes warn to dialogs.warn, which calls exec_() (dialogs.py:20-37). application_base.py:87-92 closes the viewer and returns None when add_data returns False. My probe verify-followup-2/test_modal_cancel.py (WP1 root, warn returning False) printed 'RESULT raised AttributeError 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'state''. Sizes: 370,296,000, 174,806,400 and 463,999,536 elements.
### followup-2-6 — Slider latency grows linearly with exposure index (the -TAB WCS), reaching 0.36 s per synced step
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The latency is already there on solar main without WP4: each slice change resets WCSAxes (ImageViewer._on_slice_change -> _set_wcs), and the per-point cost of the irispy -TAB pixel_to_world grows linearly with the exposure index. This is a property of the irispy/wcslib -TAB table, so the fix belongs in WP1/the loader, not WP4. WP4 sync then adds roughly 0.1 s on top of that. The linearize-or-cache remedy has not been tested.
- **Recommendation:** WP1: make slicing along the exposure axis cheap, for example by caching or linearizing the spatial -TAB table per exposure for WCSAxes. WP4 threshold: one SJI or exposure slider step with 3 synced image viewers redrawn in at most 0.25 s at any index (at least 4 fps playback) on the 1600-exposure sit-and-stare.
- **Evidence:** My rerun of test_raster_slider on the solar-main root gave 'SLIDER ... 1:0.032+0.039 ... 800:0.097+0.048 ... 1599:0.162+0.065 5:0.033+0.030'. My tabcost.py gave 0.03/0.24/0.44/0.84 ms per 1000 points at steps 1/400/800/1599 (irispy WCS ['WAVE','HPLT-TAB','HPLN-TAB']). glue/viewers/image/viewer.py:96-98. s2_quicklook.txt shows SJI frame 1 at 0.102+0.078 s and frame 399 at 0.251+0.108 s. s2_syncprofile.txt shows SYNC 0.168-0.290 and LOOKUP 0.000s.
### followup-2-8 — The performance acceptance criterion (plan lines 452-456) has no owner and no numbers
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Confirmed: the criterion only asks to 'Record' sizes, latency and memory. No WP checklist owns it and no numbers are set; the only other mention is the Dask row, which says 'First-milestone performance evidence'. The proposed thresholds are recommendations and have not been verified.

- WP3: quicklook session at most 1 MB and saved in at most 2 s.
- WP6: full 2018 window fit at most 60 s with GUI timer gaps at most 0.2 s and process-tree peak at most 12 GB.
- WP8: header scan at most 5 ms/file median; stop at most 0.5 s.

A size_probe rerun gave 67440 -> 1715976 bytes.

*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

Scaling is owned generically: first-milestone criterion plan:447 ('own wavelength grids and scaling') and the WP4 preset (plan:485, 'useful axes/scaling'). The specific histogram-opt/gamma controls are not. Movie export is tracked as unscheduled (plan:396, 1017, 1051), and later slit work includes physical distance coordinates and session support (plan:497, 619). Exposure time is a first-milestone criterion (plan:427) and the blink interval is in WP5 (plan:493). Only the playback frame range, the timing offset, derived-data export and slope/ruler tools are absent.

A grep of the plan for 'gamma|percentile|histogram|shortcut|EXPTIME|stretch|scaling' hits only :248, 447, 485, 493 and 858.
### followup-3-3 — Per-scan rasters keep -200 DN fill as data, which breaks default scaling and Mean profiles
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Per-scan rasters publish the raw flux, with -200 fill kept as data and the mask only as a separate component. The stack converts masked samples to NaN, so auto-limits and Mean profiles differ between the two load paths. The plan mentions masked samples only in the end-to-end demo (plan:453) and WP2 tests (plan:740). No WP owns display or statistics masking for per-scan data, and #53's transparent-NaN rendering cannot help because per-scan data contains no NaN.
- **Recommendation:** WP1: publish masked per-scan samples as NaN, as the stack already does, or exclude the mask from limits and statistics. Test auto-limits, Mean profiles and moment inputs on a real per-scan raster. This is a precondition for IRIS-9 3.3.1 step 2.
- **Evidence:** My independent run on scan 0 Si IV of 4000005156 printed 'n-200 3050445 frac 0.1153 nan 0', 'min at (32,400,:) -200.0 n-200 there 28', 'mask==(f==-200) True' and 'limits 99.5 -200.0 12.75' (the finding reported 11.5, which is within sampling variation). glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py:78-80; stack_spectrograms.py:53-56 (NaN). The irispy 0.8.1 mask is computed only when memmap=False (io/spectrograph.py:250-251); BAD_PIXEL_VALUE_SCALED=-200 (utils/constants.py:18).
### followup-3-6 — The WP5 blink scope misses the IRIS-9 cross-window blink (Si IV 1403 vs Mg II k 2796)
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The plan and WP5 define blink as two wavelength indices 'in one cube' (plan:447-450, 856-859). CRISPEX's IRIS-9 and Boulder blinks work because a Level-3 file concatenates windows into one spectral axis, and Level-3 is a permanent non-goal. glue-solar loads each window as a separate Data, and even with step/slit identity links the Mg II layer is disabled in a Si IV-referenced viewer. So neither the planned same-cube blink nor the WP5 layer blink meets IRIS-9 3.3.1 step 3 or Boulder Ex#7.
- **Recommendation:** WP5: define the blink as alternating (dataset, wavelength index) pairs on a shared spatial grid, for example by switching the viewer's reference data and slice, and keep the same-cube case. Test it on 4000005156 Si IV 1403 / Mg II k 2796 at about 2 fps.
- **Evidence:** crispex-replay/iris9.txt:96 (crispex on 'iris_l3_..._SiIV1403_MgIIk2796_im.fits'), :105; boulder.txt:164 (C II vs Si IV vs Mg II k3). results_iii.jsonl step 04b: mg_layer_enabled False, 'IncompatibleAttribute(Pixel Axis 2 [x])', visibility_cycle alternates. test_crispex_replay.py:249-255 adds LinkSame on pixel axes 0 and 1 only. Plan:621-622 and 1011 list Level-3 as a non-goal.
### followup-4-3 — irispy's SJI/AIA gWCS is one pixel off the FITS header
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** irispy's SJI/AIA gWCS passes the 1-based header CRPIX to dkist's VaryingCelestialTransform, which applies Shift(-crpix) to 0-based pixels. Glue's world at 0-based pixel p therefore equals the FITS world at p-1. The error is one image pixel (0.47" for the rolled binned SJI, 0.168" full-res, 0.607" AIA). The WP4 slit column (SLTPX1IX used as a 0-based column) agrees with the raster only because of this shift, so either the overlay column or the world readout will be one pixel off once the WCS is fixed. The plan does not mention this.
- **Recommendation:** Report upstream and version-gate a fix. WP1 acceptance test: per-frame corners and centre of a rolled SJI, a deconvolved SJI and an AIA cutout must match an astropy WCS built from CRPIX, CDELT, XCENIX/YCENIX and PCij_IX to within 0.05 px. WP4's slit column currently agrees with the raster (0.005") only through the shifted gWCS; retest slit_x when this is fixed.
- **Evidence:** My probe: world at 0-based CRPIX matches XCENIX/YCENIX to 1.4e-10"/6.9e-11"/1.6e-11" (rolled/decon13/aia171), and at CRPIX-1 it is off by 0.4705/0.1682/0.6067". astropy WCS(header) puts CRVAL (111.041, 79.6021") at 0-based CRPIX-1. Code: .venv irispy/io/sji.py:53; dkist 1.18.1 wcs/models.py:97 'shift = m.Shift(-crpix[0]) & m.Shift(-crpix[1])' (also upstream DKISTDC main :97); irispy origin/main sji.py:83 and gwcs_raster_clean sji.py:39. `grep -i crpix` on the plan returns nothing.
### followup-4-4 — ROI propagation to a full-resolution SJI takes about 10 s per frame
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** A raster ROI evaluated on the 32x771x1506 SJI takes about 10 s per full-resolution frame with both link_hpc and WCS autolink. A viewer-sized 600x800 fixed-resolution buffer, which is closer to what an Image Viewer draws, still takes about 4.1 s per frame, too slow for CRISPEX-style slider browsing. I did not re-measure the 338.8 s full-cube link_hpc figure, which was taken while another probe was running. The plan (lines 452-456) already requires recording latency on a larger dataset but sets no budget and has no test.
- **Recommendation:** Add a WP1/WP4 performance test on a full-resolution SJI plus raster pair with a per-frame budget. Profile the FITS -TAB world-to-pixel path before choosing link types for the quicklook preset.
- **Evidence:** My sequential runs: link_hpc (WP1, released core) s_full_frame_get_mask=10.03, s_frb_600x800=4.09/4.09; autolink (WP1 + core-combined) 9.89, 4.17/4.15; load average about 1.3-1.5. The finder's timing.jsonl shows 338.8/10.4 (link_hpc) and 10.0/9.9 (autolink).
### followup-4-5 — The browser hides deconvolved SJIs and can drop the plain file
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** scan.py:165 (and wp8_scan.py:169) keys SJIs by TDESC1, and deconvolved files carry the same plain TDESC1 ('SJI_2796', 'SJI_2832'). With both variants present, name sorting ('.' < '_') lets the deconvolved file overwrite the plain one. The browser then shows a single 'SJI_2796' entry, which loads the deconvolved cube, and the dataset label (iris.py:125) has no marker. The test tree used two names for the same deconvolved file; that a real plain file also has TDESC1=SJI_2796 is inferred from the plain SJI_1400 header following the same convention.
- **Recommendation:** Key and label SJIs with the filename suffix (for example 'SJI_2796 (deconvolved)'). WP8 test: a folder holding both variants lists and loads both, and each label shows which is which.
- **Evidence:** My scan_directory run on a tree with both names gave '4000005156 {'SJI_2796': '..._t000_deconvolved.fits.gz'} nfiles 1'. Headers: TDESC1 SJI_2796 and SJI_2832 for the two deconvolved files and SJI_1400 for the plain 4000255147 file; none has a DECON keyword. The WP8 brief (plan 953-971) has no variant handling. IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/wp9chk/browser-ux.txt:78 records that the download page offers 'SJI fits.gz (+deconvolved)'.

*sequencing_or_priority, partially_confirmed*

But the 'unpublished' part is wrong: GitHub still serves the commit and the 196-line docs/make_screenshots.py (same blob 1f640cfe), and a scratch `git fetch --depth 1` by SHA recovered it. So the helper is not lost on the next local gc, although GitHub does not promise to keep unreachable objects.

### solar-main-3 — On released glue-qt, the Pixel tool and solar:cursor_readout share the same crosshair icon
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** On released glue-qt v0.4.2, which has no `cursor_status`, setup() adds solar:cursor_readout to an ImageViewer toolbar that already holds image:point_selection. Both tools use the icon 'glue_crosshair', so the profile guide's 'the crosshair icon in the viewer toolbar' is ambiguous, and clicking the solar button only toggles the readout. The tool text tells them apart ('Pixel' vs 'Cursor readout'), so this is a UX/docs paper cut, not a functional defect. Neither the plan nor WP9 mentions it.
- **Recommendation:** Give solar:cursor_readout a distinct icon (small solar code change), or at least make WP9 name the Pixel tool by its 'Pixel' label/tooltip instead of 'the crosshair icon'.
- **Evidence:** .venv glue 1.27.0 glue/viewers/image/pixel_selection_mode.py:18-20 has icon 'glue_crosshair' with action_text 'Pixel'. glue_solar/tools.py:81-83@236f0a8 has icon 'glue_crosshair' with action_text 'Cursor readout', and its activate() toggles `shown` (lines 91-94). glue_solar/__init__.py:19-23@236f0a8 adds the tool when ImageViewer lacks cursor_status. glue-qt v0.4.2 data_viewer.py lists 'image:point_selection', and `git grep cursor_status v0.4.2` finds nothing. The guide (rst:54@236f0a8) says 'the crosshair icon'. gh release list -> v0.4.2 Latest; grep of the plan for crosshair/icon finds no mention.
### solar-main-4 — #52 is credited with raster and stack Time components that shipped in #44
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Raster (extra-coordinate) and stack `Time` components were already in #44's squash commit ccdadd3. #52's loader change only factored out `_frame_times` and added its gWCS branch, which gives SJI and AIA-cutout frames a `Time`. So plan lines 102-103 and 228-230 misattribute raster/stack Time to #52, and line 228 wrongly says `_cube_data` adds the stack's Time: it is added in `_raster_collection_data` (iris.py:112@236f0a8). The '(#52)' citations at lines 393 and 1049 are defensible, because #52 completed coverage of every dataset.
- **Recommendation:** Reword the #52 entries as 'SJI/AIA frame Time via the gWCS time axis, plus the frame-time and cursor tools'. Keep 52.feature.rst from repeating 44's raster-time entry.
- **Evidence:** git show ccdadd3:glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py lines 81-84 (raster extra_coords Time) and line 101 (stack Time in _raster_collection_data). `git diff 83efae3 2f97911 -- glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py` adds only _frame_times with its `"time" in world_axis_physical_types` branch. changelog/44.feature.rst@236f0a8 already says 'Rasters keep the exact per-exposure times'. test_importer.py@236f0a8 (~line 136) shows sdoaia171 data with 'science, mask and the per-frame Time'.
### solar-main-5 — SJI time label: Glue shows 'Time (Utc)'; plan and WP9 docs text say 'Time (UTC)'
*plan_inconsistent, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The gWCS world axis name is 'Time (UTC)', but glue-core 1.27.0's axis_label applies .title(), so the SJI world component users see is 'Time (Utc)'. Plan line 369 and WP9 line 985 give 'Time (UTC)', while line 651 gives 'Time (Utc)'. Both label lists in the plan follow WCS order, which is the reverse of Glue's world_component_ids order.
- **Recommendation:** Use the label as Glue displays it ('Time (Utc)') in the WP9 docs task and plan line 369. Note that the labels are listed in WCS order.
- **Evidence:** A scratch probe (verify-solar-main/probe_sji.py, isolated HOME) printed SJI wcs names ['Longitude', 'Latitude', 'Time (UTC)'] and Glue cids ['Time (Utc)', 'Latitude', 'Longitude']. It printed raster wcs names ['Wavelength', 'Helioprojective Latitude', 'Helioprojective Longitude'] and Glue cids ['Helioprojective Longitude', 'Helioprojective Latitude', 'Wavelength']. .venv glue/core/coordinate_helpers.py:189 returns `world_axis_names[...].title()`.

### core-qt-capabilities-6 — Collapse list is incomplete, and stock moments are range-relative, NaN-propagating and display-only
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** COLLAPSE_FUNCS is Mean, Median, Minimum, Maximum, Sum, Moment 1 and Moment 2. _on_collapse only sets an AggregateSlice on Image viewers of the same data and creates no dataset. mom1 and mom2 use the index within the selected sub-range, and np.maximum(nan, 0) propagates NaN, so one masked sample gives a NaN moment. This supports the WP2 layer_action design; the plan's list was just incomplete.
- **Recommendation:** Correct the list. Note in WP2 that stock moments are display-only, range-relative and NaN-sensitive; this supports the WP2 layer_action design.
- **Evidence:** Qt upstream/main profile_tools.py:31-37 (COLLAPSE_FUNCS) and 300-333 (_on_collapse builds AggregateSlice(slice(imin, imax), ...)). Core aggregate.py:8-26 (mom1 uses loc in range(n) over the passed subarray, with np.maximum). I ran mom1 on c[4:8] with the peak at 6 and c[5,1,1]=nan: [[2.0, 2.0], [2.0, nan]].

### upstream-state-2 — WP3 WCS serializer would not survive the raster move to gWCS
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** On gwcs_raster_clean, `_create_tabular_wcs` is gone, so WP3's FITS -TAB record path would go unused for rasters. For a single file, WP3 falls back to the 'sliced'→'asdf' record and serializes fine. Only the combined multi-file raster gWCS fails ASDF, because the custom `_InverseRasterSequenceCelestialTransform` (_spectrograph_wcs.py:65) has no converter; line 46 holds the forward `_RasterSequenceCelestialTransform`. This only matters if that unmerged branch lands, and in that case the loader breaks first (finding 1).
- **Recommendation:** Record this as a WP3 risk. Either ask irispy (the user's branch) to ship ASDF converters for its custom models, or keep a fallback that re-reads from source files via the WP3 LoadLog path instead of embedding the WCS.
- **Evidence:** `git grep -c _create_tabular_wcs` totals: v0.8.1 2, v0.9.0 2, main 2, gwcs_raster_clean 0. My probe2.py printed 'single SpectrogramCube (187, 40, 18) WCS ... ASDF ok 39369', 'multi SpectrogramCube (3, 8, 109, 17) ... world 6 ... ASDF FAILED ValidationError <_InverseRasterSequenceCelestialTransform(...)> is not of type object', 'WP3 single kind sliced asdf' and 'WP3 multi FAILED ValidationError'. The dependency comment is at IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/wp3_impl.py:36; the plan item is IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md:757-759.

The plan has no match for '2604'; lines 932-933 cite issue 2600.

### wp1-links-7 — `_converting_constructor` assumes the wrong constructor argument order
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Astropy builds objects as klass_gen(*component_values, *class_args, **kwargs, **class_kwargs), so the component values come first. The WP1 wrapper indexes values[n_args + index], which converts the wrong argument when a class tuple has positional args, and it does not handle string (keyword) component attrs. This is latent, since every IRIS WCS kind has 0 class args. `pixel_to_world_values` calling list() on a 1D scalar result fails on both main and WP1.
- **Recommendation:** Index values[index] and route string indices through kwargs, or drop the n_args handling and document that the wrapper covers IRIS WCSs only.
- **Evidence:** astropy high_level_api.py:369-390 in the .venv. WP1 loaders/iris.py:122-128. ctor_order2.py: class args () gives the correct result; args (Unit('m'),) gives 'TypeError The value must be a valid Python or Numpy numeric type'. d3_probe.py shows 0 args for the sji, raster, stack and slicedmap classes. one_d.py gives 'TypeError iteration over a 0-d array' on WP1 and on main (main loaders/iris.py:53).
### wp1-links-8 — Plan wording on how the workaround is gated differs from the prototype
*plan_inconsistent, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The prototype gates the patch on a behaviour probe and installs it when glue_solar is imported, not in setup(). The README's 'Contracts and limits' list of process-local patches names only WP3 and WP5. The plan itself already describes the behaviour-probe gate (698-699), as does README line 29. WP0 (300-303) plans to replace development gates with release floors, so the mismatch is limited to D2's 'version-gate' and the checkbox wording.
- **Recommendation:** State that WP1 uses a behaviour-probe gate with a removal note tied to the #2598 release. List WP1 among the prototypes that patch glue process-wide.
- **Evidence:** wp1/glue_solar/glue_patches.py:34-48 and wp1/glue_solar/__init__.py:6. IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/README.md:29 and :56-57. Plan lines 637-638, 698-699, 709-711 and 300-303.
### wp2-wp6-science-7 — WP6 v1 leaves out the fit window and velocity map, the parts that make fit maps usable
*sequencing_or_priority, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Plan:894 does exclude fit-window controls and velocity maps from WP6 v1. Fitting the whole Mg II window instead of the 2794-2798 Å crop raises the background by about 70 DN (40%) and lowers sigma by about 10%. The centroids, however, barely move (≤0.004 Å, about 0.4 km/s), so the claim that fit maps are unusable without a window is overstated for Mg II k. The multi-line-window impact on Si IV/C II was not measured.
- **Recommendation:** Reuse the WP2 rest/wings fields as the WP6 fit window and emit velocity (optical convention, same rest policy) in v1. Otherwise deprioritize WP6 until these land.
- **Evidence:** mgwin.py (2018 raster patch 100:160:2, 200:260:2) printed: whole n=2 'c1 2796.234 sigma1 0.096 bg 173.1 c2 2796.491' vs crop n=2 'c1 2796.230 sigma1 0.105 bg 102.7 c2 2796.498', and n=1 'c1 2796.345 ... bg 172.8' vs 'c1 2796.347 ... bg 98.8'. Gallery 07:67-69 crops 279.40-279.80 nm.
### wp2-wp6-science-8 — Saturated (Inf-flagged) samples are not caught by the WP2 saturation field, and one Inf breaks the whole WP6 fit
*science_correctness, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The code behaviour is confirmed. irispy zeroes Inf (moments.py:135) before the saturation test (moments.py:176), so an Inf-flagged pixel is not NaN'd and shows reduced intensity. In WP6, one spectrum containing Inf poisons the shared mean-spectrum seed and NaNs almost every fit. The real-data impact is unverified: local L2 rasters are int16 (BSCALE 0.25, BZERO 7992, no BLANK), a format that cannot store Inf, and all show NSATPIX=0. How saturation is actually encoded on disk is therefore unknown.
- **Recommendation:** Detect Inf, or the TSATPXn header counts, before any clipping. Mask or NaN saturated spectra in both WP2 and WP6, and warn when TSATPXn > 0. Test with a synthetic Inf sample.
- **Evidence:** inf2.py printed 'saturation_limit=1e6: Inf pixel I 422.9 normal 659.9 Inf pixel NaN? False' and 'WP6 with 3 Inf samples in 1 of 18 spectra: finite centroid count 1 / 18'. The header probe printed '16 0.25 7992 None' plus NSATPIX/TSATPX1-7 = 0. ITN 26 section 6.4 (https://iris.lmsal.com/itn26/data_notes.html) says 'Saturated pixels are flagged as Inf'.
### wp2-wp6-science-9 — Intensities are in DN, not DN/s: variable exposures (AEC) are ignored in maps and thresholds
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The briefs publish DN, never mention exposure time, and the loader keeps per-exposure times in meta (irispy io/spectrograph.py:201-228). However, ITN 26 says varied AEC exposures occur 'only ... in extreme cases' such as flares. The local 2018 raster has constant 7.999 s exposures apart from one 0-s FUV step, and that step is already fully masked (-200), so masking covers it. Of the 7278 fully missing Si IV spectra, 548 come from that step. DN/s normalisation is a sensible option but not demonstrated to be necessary.
- **Recommendation:** Offer DN/s normalization using meta['exposure time'] (default on for the intensity/amplitude maps) and set 0-s steps to NaN. Document that thresholds are applied after normalization.
- **Evidence:** exp0.py printed 'Si IV 1403 exp-0 steps [157] fully masked there: [True] unique exp [0.0, 7.999]' and 'Mg II k 2796 ... unique exp [7.999]'. ITN 26 calibration page: 'This is the default for most active region observations, although different exposures are only used in extreme cases when very bright phenomena can lead to CCD saturation (e.g. a flare).'
### wp2-wp6-science-11 — No absolute wavelength-calibration caveat or offset for velocity products
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The plan offers velocity products (WP2) and Doppler display (WP5) relative to a rest wavelength, with no caveat about absolute wavelength calibration and no offset option. ITN 26 section 5.1 and ITN 38 section 4.2 both require a manual check against reference lines. I downgraded severity because the fix is a documentation caveat plus an optional offset and does not block quicklook use.
- **Recommendation:** Document the caveat in the product metadata and the guide. Allow an optional reference-line velocity offset (the WP5 override can carry it) instead of implying absolute velocities.
- **Evidence:** grep -n -i 'calibrat' IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md found no wavelength-calibration caveat. ITN 26 (https://iris.lmsal.com/itn26/calibration.html) section 5.1: 'accurate to only a few pixels, and should be manually checked ... Ni I 279.9474 nm ... O I 135.560 nm'. ITN 38 section 4.2: 'within an uncertainty of 5–10 km s-1'.
### wp2-wp6-science-12 — Full-size float64 residual attached to the source doubles memory and gets embedded in sessions
*overengineering, confirmed*

- **Verified:** fit_gaussians converts to float64, so the residual stored on the source is float64 at the source's full shape: 533 MB against a 266.5 MB float32 source for a 320x548x380 Mg II window. A component without _load_log is saved by value, so the residual would be embedded in a session. I confirmed the embedding by reading the code, not by saving a session.
- **Recommendation:** Make the residual opt-in and float32, or let users compare model and data on a selected pixel via the Profile Fit tab instead.
- **Evidence:** mgwin.py printed 'dtype float32 (320, 548, 380)'; 320*548*380*4 = 266.5 MB and *8 = 533.1 MB. stack4d.py printed 'residual ... float64'. .venv glue/core/state.py:1093-1101 (_save_component returns data=... unless _load_log). Plan:886.
### wp2-wp6-science-13 — Width convention (sigma) is never stated
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** WP2 'width'/'velocity_width' and WP6 'width' are all 1-sigma standard deviations, and the plan never states the convention. ITN 38 works in FWHM = 2√(2 ln 2) σ. I did not verify the 1/e-width usage the finding cites.
- **Recommendation:** Name the components 'sigma' or 'sigma_velocity', or document the convention in the component metadata and the guide.
- **Evidence:** grep -n -i -E 'sigma|standard deviation|stddev|fwhm|1/e' IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md found nothing. irispy 0.8.1 moments.py:79 ('standard deviation') and 195-199. wp6_fitting.py:111 maps stddev_i to 'width'. ITN 38 quotes 'w_FWHM = 2 sqrt(2ln2) sigma'.
### wp2-wp6-science-14 — WP2 real-data test and 'compare with irispy' check nothing physical
*prototype_not_useful, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The irispy 0.8.1 bundled Mg II window (2793.44-2794.74 Å) contains no line core, and IN_WINDOW_REST 2794.09 is its midpoint, so '|median v| < 20' is a plumbing check. The WP6 synthetic cubes are noiseless. The prototypes README already concedes that physical validation is absent (README:30, 35). Under irispy 0.9.0 the bundled windows are full windows at a 10x-decimated 0.254 Å step, so the 'no line core' premise is specific to 0.8.1.
- **Recommendation:** Add a synthetic SpectrogramCube with a known Gaussian, including noise, missing samples and Inf, and assert centroid, sigma and velocity. Keep real rasters for shape and unit checks only.
- **Evidence:** test_wp2_moments.py:13 and 62. wp6_test_fitting.py:23-38 has no noise. fit_test.py printed 'Mg II k 2796 (187, 40, 52) wl 2793.44-2794.74'. win090.py printed for 0.9.0 'Mg II k 2796 ... wl 2793.42-2806.40 step 0.25460' and 'Si IV 1403 ... wl 1398.63-1405.75 step 0.25440'.
### wp2-wp6-science-15 — The '3D/4D shape contract' for WP6 is tested only for 3D in the recorded suite
*plan_incorrect, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The recorded 17-test suite has no 4D stack case for WP6; the only 4D evidence is the historical probe log. When I ran the current prototype on a 4D stack it did work (maps (13,8,109), 3 links), so this is a coverage and wording gap, not a functional one.
- **Recommendation:** Move a 4D stack case into wp6_test_fitting.py, or reword the claim.
- **Evidence:** grep -n -i -E 'stack|4d|ndim' wp6_test_fitting.py review_20260905/test_plan_integration.py found nothing. wp6_chk_real_fixed.log:21-23. The recorded command printed '17 passed' (tests.log:16). stack4d.py printed 'stack (13, 8, 109, 17) maps (13, 8, 109) residual (13, 8, 109, 17) float64 links 3'.
### wp2-wp6-science-16 — Product meta keys do not match the WP5 rest-wavelength helper
*plan_inconsistent, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The WP5 helper reads meta['rest_wavelength'] or SGMeta.rest_wavelength. WP6 maps store 'rest_wavelength_angstrom', so the helper returns None and velocity conversion is unavailable. WP2 maps inherit SGMeta, so the helper returns TWAVE and ignores the rest wavelength the user entered.
- **Recommendation:** Write the rest actually used under the key WP5 reads, on both the WP2 and WP6 products.
- **Evidence:** metak.py printed 'WP2 maps meta SGMeta user rest 2794.09 -> WP5 helper 2796.1999511699996 Angstrom' and 'WP6 maps meta {... 'rest_wavelength_angstrom': 2794.09} -> WP5 helper None'. wp6_fitting.py:105. wp5_units.py:11-24.
### wp3-wp5-5 — The global Quantity saver makes any session with Quantity metadata require glue-solar
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** wp3_impl.py:102-110 registers a process-wide Quantity saver/loader. A non-IRIS session with Quantity metadata saved under it will not open in plain glue (KeyError 'data'), and Quantity subclasses come back as plain Quantity. The plan already asks for 'scoped style/Quantity serialization' (plan:750), so this is a gap between the prototype and the plan, not a plan error. Stock 1.27.0 cannot save such metadata at all (TypeError), and #2597 is still open.
- **Recommendation:** Upstream a Quantity saver to glue-core (small PR next to #2597), or limit it to IRIS metadata. Record the portability trade-off in the WP3 brief.
- **Evidence:** q_save.py printed 'save-stock SAVE FAILED TypeError no implementation found for numpy.save', 'save-wp3 saved', "load-wp3 restored meta types {'exptime': 'Quantity', 'lat': 'Quantity'}" and "load-plain LOAD FAILED KeyError 'data'". gh pr view 2597 shows OPEN.
### wp3-wp5-9 — The 11-line list omits standard IRIS lines and wavelength-calibration references
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** iris_lines.csv has 11 lines and omits Fe XII 1349.40, whose window is in the test raster (TDESC3 'Fe XII 1349'). It also omits the Mg II triplet (vacuum 2798.75/2798.82), S IV 1404.81/1406.02 and O IV 1404.78. Young, Tian & Jaeggli (2015) give Fe XXI 1354.106±0.023 Å against the list's 1354.08, although ITN 38 itself labels it 1354.08. The labels are inconsistent ('C II 1335' where the TDESC is 'C II 1336'). This is a label list, so omissions do not corrupt numbers, hence low.
- **Recommendation:** Add Fe XII 1349.40, the Mg II triplet, O IV 1404.78, S IV 1406.02, Ni I 2799.474 and S I 1401.515. Use 3-decimal NIST/CHIANTI values with a source column and consistent names matching TDESC.
- **Evidence:** The NIST ASD query returned Mg II air 2797.930/2797.998 (vacuum about 2798.75/2798.82), S IV 1404.808/1406.009, and Fe XII 1349.40 (obs). The ApJ 799 218 abstract (WebFetch) gives '1354.106 ± 0.023 Å'. twave.py printed 'Fe XII 1349 1349.43'.
### wp3-wp5-10 — The velocity display ignores the Level-2 wavelength-calibration caveat
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** ITN 38 §4.2 says the Level-2 wavelength calibration should always be checked manually against strong neutral lines, stable to within 5-10 km/s. It names O I (printed as '1335.60', evidently 1355.60), S I 1401.515 and Ni I 2799.474. The plan's WP5 has no disclosure and no per-dataset offset. For a quicklook this is mainly a docs/UI caveat, hence low.
- **Recommendation:** State in the UI and docs that velocities are relative to the uncorrected Level-2 scale. Optionally add a per-dataset wavelength offset, stored as plain metadata, next to the rest override.
- **Evidence:** curl of iris.lmsal.com/itn38/analysis_lines_iris.html, text line 108 (quoted). grep of the plan for 'calibrat|2799|Ni I|S I 1401' finds only lines 495 and 898, which are unrelated to the WP5 velocity scale.
### wp3-wp5-12 — The UI entry points for the rest override and the line list were dropped
*dropped_from_archive, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The current WP5 brief requires an 'explicit Angstrom override' and a packaged line list, but it names no UI entry point and does not reference the existing wp5_override.py prototype. The archived plan had a 'Set rest wavelength…' layer_action, a 'Plugins -> IRIS: add line list' menubar action, and docs quantifying the 16 km/s Mg II k offset.
- **Recommendation:** Restore both actions, and the prototype reference, to the WP5 checklist.
- **Evidence:** grep of the plan for 'Set rest|add line list|wp5_override' finds nothing. The WP5 references are at plan:833-837. The archive has these at archive/2026-09-05/IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md:3413, 3622, 3739, 3741 and 3761. IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/wp5_override.py:13 contains the layer_action.
### wp3-wp5-13 — CRISPEX blink/playback modes and scaling are only partly mapped
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** CRISPEX has LOOP/CYCLE (ping-pong)/BLINK temporal playback modes, spectral blink between lp_blink and lp_blink_init, main/reference blink, and image scaling modes 'based on first/current/per time step'. glue-qt playback only goes forward or back, with wrap. The plan maps spectral and layer blink but not CYCLE or temporal BLINK, and it does not make the scaling policy an option.
- **Recommendation:** List the temporal-blink and cycle modes as deferred or accepted gaps, and make the scaling policy (shared vs per-position) an explicit option of the two-wavelength blink.
- **Evidence:** crispex.pro:13601-13624 (CYCLE/BLINK), :13571-13574 (spectral blink toggle), :13859-13880, :4221-4230 and :7950-7955 (imagescale modes). ~/Git/glue-qt glue_qt/viewers/common/data_slice_widget.py:81-89 and :146-155 (back/stop/forw).
### wp3-wp5-14 — WP3 sliced-WCS record crashes on NumPy integer slice indices
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** wp3_impl.py:65 uses isinstance(s, int), so a SlicedLowLevelWCS built with a numpy integer index fails to serialize.
- **Recommendation:** Use isinstance(s, numbers.Integral) and int(s), and also cast slice start, stop and step to int.
- **Evidence:** A probe printed 'int OK [0, [None, None, None], [None, None, None]]' and then "int64 FAILED AttributeError 'numpy.int64' object has no attribute 'start'".
### wp3-wp5-15 — The line-list style-editor patch hides editors for other Profile artists
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** glue-qt's on_artist_add looks up the artist class exactly in a dict and hits 'else: return' on a miss. That drops the style editor for unknown or subclassed Profile artists, and also for any later artists in the same batch. wp5_linelist_mod.py:116-118 turns ProfileViewer's single editor class into such a dict.
- **Recommendation:** Use a dict subclass with a ProfileLayerStyleEditor fallback, or scope the change to IRIS artists.
- **Evidence:** ~/Git/glue-qt glue_qt/core/layer_artist_model.py:364-369 (the same on origin/main). ProfileViewer._layer_style_widget_cls = ProfileLayerStyleEditor at glue_qt/viewers/profile/data_viewer.py:19.
### wp3-wp5-16 — The blink tool puts a permanent spinbox in every Image viewer toolbar
*prototype_not_useful, confirmed*

- **Verified:** 'image:blink' is appended to ImageViewer.tools globally (wp5_blink.py:67-68), and the tool's __init__ adds the interval QSpinBox to viewer.toolbar (line 38). So every Image viewer shows the spinbox. The blinked layer set is frozen at activation (line 48 comment).
- **Recommendation:** Show the interval control only while blink is active, or keep one fixed or settable interval.
- **Evidence:** wp5_blink.py:31-38, :48 and :67-68, read directly. This was not run in a GUI.
### wp3-wp5-17 — The WCSAxes line position uses a brentq root find where interpolation would do
*overengineering, confirmed*

- **Verified:** _world_to_profile_pixels already samples a finite, strictly monotonic trace (wp5_linelist_mod.py:90-95), so np.interp on the sorted trace would replace the per-line brentq, which calls the full WCS at each iteration. The result is exact for a linear spectral axis. IRIS wavelength axes are linear WAVE; -TAB is used only for pointing.
- **Recommendation:** Replace it with np.interp(value, trace_sorted, pixels_sorted).
- **Evidence:** wp5_linelist_mod.py:85-100. The raster WCS ctype is ['WAVE', 'HPLT-TAB', 'HPLN-TAB'] (moments_save.py output).
### wp3-wp5-18 — The -TAB rebuild depends on irispy internals that a WIP gWCS branch replaces
*plan_stale, confirmed*

- **Verified:** irispy #159 (FITS-TAB pointing) merged at 2026-08-05T02:20Z. origin/gwcs_raster_clean is 12 commits ahead, last commit 2026-09-08, uses _create_raster_gwcs/_combine_raster_cubes, and has no PR. Also missed by the plan: irispy v0.9.0 was released 2026-09-10 and the plan only names 0.8.1. 0.9.0 still uses _create_tabular_wcs and a TWAVE-based rest_wavelength, but now returns None when TWAVE is missing.
- **Recommendation:** Have WP3 dispatch on WCS capability, not layout. Note in the plan that irispy raster WCS may change and that the ASDF path must handle any custom irispy transforms.
- **Evidence:** gh pr view 159 --repo LM-SAL/irispy gives mergedAt 2026-08-05T02:20:20Z. git rev-list --count main..gwcs_raster_clean = 12. gh pr list --head gwcs_raster_clean is empty. gh release list shows v0.9.0 2026-09-10. git grep v0.9.0 -- irispy/io/spectrograph.py:56 _create_tabular_wcs. grep '0\.9' of the plan finds nothing.
### wp3-wp5-19 — The session-size test threshold is looser than the brief
*plan_inconsistent, confirmed*

- **Verified:** wp3chk_test_sessions.py:131 asserts len(s) < 200_000, while the brief (plan:771-772) targets under 100 KB. The prototype suite passes (9 passed with test_line_positions), but I did not re-measure the 58,439-byte figure.
- **Recommendation:** Tighten the assertion to the documented 100 KB target, or state the looser bound in the brief.
- **Evidence:** wp3chk_test_sessions.py:131, read directly. The runner (HOME=scratch, run_checks.py 'wp1:protos' test_line_positions.py wp3chk_test_sessions.py) printed '9 passed, 2 warnings'. git -C ~/Git/glue-solar status --short was empty afterwards.
### wp4-quicklook-3 — Slit overlay from raw SLTPX1IX never appears on the matched fixture; pixel_stride fixes the wrong problem
*science_correctness, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The headline is wrong: the fixtures are stride-10 decimations, not crops, so pixel_stride=10 does put the slit on the image. SLTPX1IX 187 becomes 18.7, next to CRPIX1 18.45 on the 37-px SJI; 2023 values 549/1269 become 54.9/126.9 on the 178-px SJI. The WCS projection to x≈21-23 is an artifact: the fixture keeps native CDELT after decimation. What remains valid: the SLTPX1IX 0- vs 1-based convention is unresolved (CRISPEX uses it as CRPIX, ITN41 plots it unshifted), and irispy tags these pixel values as arcsec.
- **Recommendation:** Derive the overlay as CRISPEX does: raster slit world coordinates at the matched exposure, projected through the SJI WCS of the matched frame. Use SLTPX1IX only as a cross-check after the base convention is confirmed on full-size real data. Drop pixel_stride from the production API and handle fixture decimation in tests.
- **Evidence:** astropy header dump: 2021 SJI shape (62,40,37), CDELT1 0.16635, CRPIX1 18.45, FOVX 61.2168 (≈368 native px); raster ext (187,40,*) CDELT2 0.16635, FOVY 66.54; 2023 SJI (2,109,178), CRPIX1 88.8, SLTPX1IX [548.99, 1268.71]. The archived wp4_common.py comment says 'irispy's bundled test SJIs are stride-10 decimations'. crispex.pro:11046-11047 and 11077-11078 (SLTPX1IX used as crpix), 11757-11760 (WCS_GET_PIXEL). ITN41 cell: plt.axvline(x=sji[1].data[timestep, 4]). irispy/io/sji.py:212 (* u.arcsec).
### wp4-quicklook-5 — WP4 brief omits the temporal-master, visible-offset, no-match and exposure-time criteria
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The plan does assign these to WP4: the feature table (plan:491) says 'choose the driving dataset, compute nearest exposure plus signed offset, define gap/no-match/tie rules', and sequence step 4 (plan:591-595) repeats it. It is not only step 4. The gap is that the WP4 checklist (plan:779-829) omits master/offset display and exposure-time readout, and names no concrete no-match rule. Prototype facts hold: nearest returns indices only and clamps, whichever slider fires drives, and 'actual exposure time' (plan:427) is ambiguous.
- **Recommendation:** Add to the WP4 brief: a user-selectable master (dataset plus axis); nearest returning (index, signed offset); a concrete no-match rule (for example |offset| > half the master cadence or exposure overlap, shown as 'no match' rather than clamped); and offset/timestamp readouts built on FrameTimeTool. State whether 'exposure time' means timestamp, duration or both.
- **Evidence:** grep in plan: 437-438, 491, 592, 799-802. wp4_common.py:37-49 (np.clip clamp, indices only) and 106-131. test_refresh.py:14-17 asserts [0,0,1] for out-of-coverage queries. crispex.pro:875-895 and 2858-2912 (explicit master_time 0/1/2 with nearest selection).
### wp4-quicklook-14 — quicklook treats sit-and-stare like a raster and calls its time–slit image a 'map'
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The sns raster has STEPS_AV 0, NRASTERP 1 and CDELT3 0. Its axis-0 world component is 'Helioprojective Longitude', so its step slider carries a spatial label although the axis is time. quicklook applies the same step × slit 'map' preset with no branch for sit-and-stare, although the plan distinguishes the two cases (plan:443-444, 525-526, 452-455).
- **Recommendation:** Branch the preset on STEPS_AV/NRASTERP. For sit-and-stare, open slit × time and time × wavelength and label the axis as exposure/time.
- **Evidence:** Header dump: raster STEPS_AV 0.0, NRASTERP 1, OBS_DESC 'Medium sit-and-stare', ext CDELT3 0.0. test_v_label.py: world cids ['Helioprojective Longitude', 'Helioprojective Latitude', 'Wavelength']. wp4_common.py:185-194 has no STEPS_AV/NRASTERP check.
### wp4-quicklook-15 — Recorded tests do not exercise link_time, whisker, quicklook viewer setup or slit overlay; one probe is stale
*prototype_test_failure, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The recorded WP4 checks do not cover the real behaviour. test_plan_integration calls quicklook(app, []) with new_data_viewer monkeypatched to None, so only link_hpc is exercised. test_refresh covers nearest, synthetic slit_x, observation_key, the add_time no-op and single-dataset sync cleanup. Cross-dataset time mapping, whisker, viewer setup and the slit overlay are untested. The historical wp4_chk_join.py now crashes at line 54.
- **Recommendation:** Before porting, turn wp4_e_timelink and wp4_f_sync assertions into pytest cases on the real fixtures, and add the order, Pixel-path, AggregateSlice and V34 regressions above.
- **Evidence:** review_20260905/test_plan_integration.py:22-41; test_refresh.py:14-67; plan:256 ('WP1/WP4 ... 4 passed'). Isolated run of wp4_chk_join.py: 'line 54 ... dc.remove_link([l for l in links if isinstance(l, JoinLink)][0]) IndexError: list index out of range'. git status of glue-solar stayed clean.
### wp4-quicklook-16 — Dead add_time and fixture-only pixel_stride in the porting base
*overengineering, confirmed*

- **Verified:** add_time is a guarded no-op, because the loader already adds Time. Only unselected historical probes call it; quicklook does not. pixel_stride exists only for the stride-10 decimated irispy fixtures, as the archived prototype comment states. Keeping fixture scaling in tests is simpler. That real L2 aux values are always in stored-array pixels (for example with SUMSPAT>1) is supported only by the header comment, not verified on real data.
- **Recommendation:** Delete add_time and pixel_stride from the port and keep fixture adjustments in tests.
- **Evidence:** wp4_common.py:10-13 and 16-22; wp1 iris.py:172-175 adds Time; test_refresh.py:45-52; grep shows add_time used only in wp4_c_slit.py:19 and wp4_g_slit_loader.py:14 (plus wp4_chk_join.py). The archived wp4_common.py comment says 'bundled test SJIs are stride-10 decimations'. aux header comment: 'Slit center in X of each exposure in window-pixe'.
### wp7-wp8-3 — WP8 Stop, Cancel, close and rescan freeze the GUI for the whole directory walk
*plan_incorrect, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The freeze reproduces: scan_directory sorts the whole rglob/is_file walk before it first polls stop (wp8_scan.py:136-141), and _stop_scan calls worker.wait() on the GUI thread (wp8_proto.py:51). However, the plan is not unaware of this. Lines 498 and 968-969 explicitly record that the walk is not interruptible. The inaccuracy is only that 'delay stop' understates the effect, which is a GUI-wide freeze. It matters only for large or network trees; polling stop inside the walk is the cheap fix.
- **Recommendation:** Poll stop() inside the enumeration, e.g. collect paths in a loop that breaks on stop(), then sort. Say in the brief that wait() runs on the GUI thread, so a stop that is not cooperative freezes the application rather than just delaying.
- **Evidence:** Scratch test_f3_reject_blocks_during_walk, with Path.is_file slowed to 0.1 s on the iris_tree fixture: 'reject blocked 1.16s, is_file calls 11', 1 passed. Plan 498: 'document that directory enumeration/current header reads can delay stop'. Plan 968-969: 'initial sorted directory walk is not interruptible in this prototype'.
### wp7-wp8-4 — WP8 partial results after Stop list truncated observations with no marker
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** After a stop, an observation can list only some of its rasters, and neither the row nor the Observation dataclass (wp8_scan.py:54-73) marks it as incomplete. So the claim of 'no marker' is true per row. However, the dialog does show a global 'Scan stopped' label (wp8_proto.py:52), and the user triggered the stop. Plan 958 ('Keep grouped/sorted partial results') does not address per-observation completeness, which is worth a sentence in the brief.
- **Recommendation:** Mark observations that may be incomplete after a stop (e.g. a '(partial scan)' suffix or a disabled Stack option), or drop observations whose files were only partly seen. Test it.
- **Evidence:** Scratch test_f4_truncated_after_stop: 'full {... '3400109360': 2} partial {... '3400109360': 1}', 1 passed. wp8_scan.py:54-73 has no completeness field. wp8_proto.py:52 sets 'Scan stopped'.
### wp7-wp8-6 — WP7 GOES satellite rule is unspecified, and GOES-19 is unreachable with released sunpy
*plan_stale, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Several details are confirmed. Plan 920-921 does not state a satellite rule, and the prototype takes the first satellite (context_chk.py:43-44). GOES-19 is unreachable with released sunpy, and live SatelliteNumber values are int64 while the mock uses strings (test_context.py:65). However, NOAA's current X-ray primary is GOES-18, with GOES-19 as secondary, so post-2025 searches still return the primary XRS satellite and the missing GOES-19 is not a functional gap today. The finding's claims about GOES-19 becoming operational GOES-East in April 2025 and GOES-16 being turned off are not supported by the cited SWPC page.
- **Recommendation:** State the rule in the brief (for example, prefer the NOAA primary or let the user choose with a.goes.SatelliteNumber). Note that GOES-19 needs sunpy>=8.1. Mock with int64 satellite numbers.
- **Evidence:** goes.py:183 goes_number=[2..18]. gh pr view 8719: MERGED 2026-08-20, milestone 8.1.0. gh release list: v8.0.0 Latest. Live Fido.search results: 2025-06-10 [18] int64, 2021-09-05 [16, 17] int64, 2026-09-01 [18]. https://services.swpc.noaa.gov/json/goes/instrument-sources.json (2026-09-22): "xrays": {"secondary": 19, "primary": 18}. WebFetch of spaceweather.gov/products/goes-x-ray-flux: no mapping and no GOES-16/19 dates stated.
### wp7-wp8-7 — WP7 GOES dates are wrong on every released core; the implementation queue omits the #2599 gate
*sequencing_or_priority, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The date bug is real on released core 1.27.0, and #2599 is still an open draft. Only the queue row (plan 688) omits the gate. The brief already treats it as a gate. Line 914 says 'exact datetime plotting against core #2599 remain separate gates'. Lines 924-927 say 'A necessary older-core workaround must be gated'. Line 934 already requires 'exact date labels' in tests. So the recommended test assertion is already in the brief.
- **Recommendation:** Add 'core #2599 released, or gated epoch workaround' to the WP7 queue row. Add a date-label assertion to the WP7 tests.
- **Evidence:** Ran wp0_bug_epoch_viewer.py with HOME isolated: x tick labels ['06 00:20',...], xlim ['3990-09-06','3990-09-06']. Scratch GOES success test ticks were ['09-06 01', ...] for 2021-09-05 data. gh pr view 2599 -R glue-viz/glue: OPEN, isDraft true. Plan lines 688, 914, 924-927 and 934.
### wp7-wp8-8 — WP7 GOES success path (viewer, log axis, time link) is untested, and one comment is stale
*other, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Confirmed. The pytest files in wp7chk exercise goes_context only through the failure test (test_context.py:144-162). The standalone probes wp7_goes.py and wp7_timelink.py exercised the Scatter viewer and the time link outside pytest. The success path works. The comment at context_chk.py:150 is stale, because SJI datasets have carried a Time component since PR #52 (commit 5fca797, loaders/iris.py:81-96).
- **Recommendation:** Port the success-path test with datetime64 range bounds, and fix the comment.
- **Evidence:** Scratch test_v_goes.py::test_goes_success, with goes_xrs monkeypatched and the wp1 and wp7chk roots: 'viewer ScatterViewer y_log True', 'SJI frames selected [0]', passed. git merge-base shows 5fca797 ('Attach the UTC time of every SJI frame') is in #52 merge 2f97911.
### wp7-wp8-10 — 'Faster observation browsing' (WP8) does not reduce scan work
*plan_inconsistent, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** It is accurate that WP8 does not reduce scan work. wp8_scan reads every supported FITS header (wp8_scan.py:151-152), while windows come only from the first raster (176-177), and iris_xfiles reads one header per group (fitsfiles[ind[0]]). The label 'Faster observation browsing' (plan 498) can reasonably mean faster browsing for the user, through the filter and a non-blocking, stoppable scan, and WP8's own heading is 'Browser filter and cooperative stop'. So this is a cosmetic wording issue.
- **Recommendation:** Reword the row as filter and stop. Optionally skip header reads for SPEC files after the first raster of an observation that is already keyed by filename; this is a cheap speed-up for long repeated rasters.
- **Evidence:** wp8_scan.py:139-177. Plan 498 and the WP8 heading. WebFetch of https://hesperia.gsfc.nasa.gov/ssw/iris/idl/uio/ql/iris_xfiles.pro: ssw_time2paths(tstartval, tstopval, ...) and mreadfits_header, fitsfiles[ind[0]], ... 'one header per observation group'.
### wp7-wp8-11 — WP8 shows 'Scan stopped' for a complete scan
*prototype_bug, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Reproduced. If the worker has finished but its result is still queued, clicking Stop sets 'Scan stopped' (wp8_proto.py:48-52). The full result then populates, and _scan_over never resets the format (54-62).
- **Recommendation:** Set the text only if worker.isFinished() was false when Stop was requested, or set it in _scanned based on the stop flag.
- **Evidence:** Scratch test_f11_stop_after_finish, using worker.wait() then stop_scan.click(): 'rows 4 format Scan stopped', 1 passed.
### wp7-wp8-12 — WP8 stop and rescan tests still rely on a 0.2 s sleep window
*prototype_test_failure, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Confirmed by reading the code. The tests do gate the start of stop on a counted read (waitUntil len>=1). However, the assertions len(slow_headers)==1 (wp8_test_proto.py:106, 147) hold only if Stop or reject reaches the worker within the 0.2 s sleep (lines 43-46), so they still depend on timing. No failure was observed: the six recorded runs all passed.
- **Recommendation:** Block the fake read on a threading.Event that the test releases after clicking Stop, so the gate depends on read counts only.
- **Evidence:** wp8_test_proto.py:38-49, 96-106 and 141-147. wp8_chk_run1-3.log: '30 passed'. wp8_chk_fixrun1-3.log: '31 passed'.
### wp7-wp8-13 — WP8 filter misses window/SJI names and gives no hint about hidden ticked items
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** _apply_filter matches only top-level columns 0-2 (wp8_proto.py:78-82). Window, SJI and AIA names live in child rows (loaders/iris.py:240-258), or in column 6 for single-entry rows, so they are not matched. Hidden ticked items load with no count shown. However, this is exactly the scope the plan specifies at 953-956 ('STARTOBS/OBSID/description', 'Hidden ticked observations still load'), so the prototype is not wrong. Matching child text is an optional enhancement, and the claim that browsing is 'line-window driven' is not evidenced.
- **Recommendation:** Also match child text, which is a one-line change. Show a count of hidden selected items next to Load.
- **Evidence:** wp8_proto.py:78-82. loaders/iris.py:240-258 and 251. Plan 953-956. test_filter_survives_rescan_and_hidden_ticks_still_load (wp8_test_proto.py:85-93) asserts the specified behaviour.

### plan-integrity-22 — 'Actual exposure time' per panel is ambiguous for IRIS
*science_correctness, confirmed*

- **Verified:** Milestone L427 'Show each panel's actual exposure time' is ambiguous. In irispy, 'exposure time' is the per-exposure integration duration, while the plan's context (L436-446, L530-532) means acquisition timestamps. A raster-map panel spans one timestamp per step, not a single time.
- **Recommendation:** Reword to 'show the acquisition timestamp (and time range for raster maps) of each displayed frame'. Add EXPTIME separately if wanted.
- **Evidence:** IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md L427, L444-446, L530-532; .venv irispy/io/spectrograph.py:228 meta.add('exposure time', exposure_times, ...) alongside :215 't_obs = aux_times + exposure_times_fuv / 2' (duration) and :273 extra_coords.add('time', ...).

### archive-trace-13 — WP6 writes a rest-wavelength key that WP5 does not read
*prototype_bug, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The key mismatch is real: WP6 writes `rest_wavelength_angstrom` and WP5 reads only `rest_wavelength` or SGMeta, so a fit map's centroid would never offer km/s through the image layer's attribute_display_unit. However, the current plan does not claim that fit maps feed velocity display (894-895 keep velocity-derived maps out of v1). Also, the WP6 value is the optional fit seed centre (None when left blank), not a rest wavelength. Simply renaming it to `rest_wavelength` would label a fit seed as a rest wavelength.
- **Recommendation:** Use one meta key across WP2/WP5/WP6 (`rest_wavelength`, float in Angstrom).
- **Evidence:** wp6_fitting.py:47-49, 65-66, 105 and 136-146 (the dialog says 'Line centre', blank means seed from the data); wp6_fitting_brief.py:106; wp5_units.py:11-23. The venv glue viewers/image/state.py:500-642 has attribute_display_unit. Archive 3869 and 4657 made the WP6 -> WP5 claim.

### usefulness-5 — Do not port the app.new_data_viewer wrapper; the plan both endorses and disowns it

### usefulness-8 — Other global-patch prototypes to port only as scoped code (WP3, WP1)
*prototype_bug, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Importing wp3_impl does register a process-global `@saver(u.Quantity)`/`@loader` and patches `_GlueWCS.__gluestate__`/`__setgluestate__` and `L/M.VisualAttributes`. `L.image_data`/`L.raster_data` are rebound only by the explicit `install_browser_factories()`, not on import. A duplicate saver would not 'silently shadow': `VersionedDict.__setitem__` raises KeyError('Cannot overwrite version 1 ...'), so a future core Quantity saver would make glue-solar's import fail. The prototype uses a SolarVisualAttributes subclass, not a global VisualAttributes saver, which matches plan 664-666. The WP1 patch is behavior-gated and carries a removal note, as plan 709-711 and D2 require. The real gap is only that the plan does not say whether the Quantity saver belongs in core or solar.
- **Recommendation:** Port the WP3 logic as `_GlueWCS.__gluestate__`/`__setgluestate__` methods and named factory functions in loaders/iris.py. Send the Quantity saver to core as a PR, with a version-gated solar fallback. Keep the WP1 patch gated, add a removal note tied to the #2598 release floor, and never import the prototype modules into production.
- **Evidence:** wp3_impl.py:102-110, 113-127, 159-169. Installed glue/core/state.py:216-239 has the overwrite check. Probe printed 'SECOND KeyError "Cannot overwrite version 1 of <class 'astropy.units.quantity.Quantity'>"'. wp1/glue_solar/glue_patches.py:11 (removal note) and 46-48 (gated).
### usefulness-9 — The plan says it is local and untracked, but it is committed and public on origin/plan
*plan_stale, confirmed*

Of those tracked files, 48 contain /Users/nabil or /private/tmp paths. Lines 1070-1071 are a dated 2026-09-22 statement and remain accurate.

### usefulness-11 — No launch path for the quicklook
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

Nothing calls wp4 `quicklook(app, datasets)` except probes and a stubbed test, and the plan never names glue-qt's `startup_action` (`glue --startup=`). The plan does specify a launch path, however: WP4 'Port ... browser quicklook' (794), D4's menu-plugin path (644-645), and 'fixed-layout registration' / 'Reuse the existing layout machinery' (485). What is missing is a CLI/startup entry comparable to CRISPEX's one-line call (confirmed in the IRIS-9 3.3.2 text), not any launch path at all.
- **Recommendation:** Add an M0 requirement with one entry point per context: an 'Open quicklook' button in the browser, a menubar action on the selected datasets, and a registered `startup_action` for `glue --startup=iris_quicklook files...`. A Python `glue_solar.quicklook(paths)` is optional.

### usefulness-17 — No keyboard or scaling parity with CRISPEX, even though registries exist
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** CRISPEX defines the key and mouse shortcuts listed in the finding: Shift+B/F to step frames, Shift+Backspace/Space/Tab to play or pause, Shift+A/S and Ctrl+A/S to change the main and reference positions, and left/middle/right click to lock, measure and unlock. glue-qt's keyboard_shortcut registry has only Tab and Backspace registered, and glue's stretches are linear/sqrt/arcsinh/log, with no gamma. The plan mentions neither. This is optional polish, given the plan's 'no full IDL widget clone' non-goal (1012).
- **Recommendation:** Add an optional M1 item: register a minimal frame and wavelength step/play keymap for preset viewers and a power (gamma) stretch through the public registries, with no new UI.
- **Evidence:** grviss/crispex crispex.pro:9915-9937 (scratch clone, also raw.githubusercontent.com master lines 9915-9925). ~/Git/glue-qt/glue_qt/app/keyboard_shortcuts.py:32 and 43. `.venv` probe printed 'STRETCHES ['linear', 'sqrt', 'arcsinh', 'log']'.
### usefulness-18 — The slit_x pixel_stride production contract exists only for decimated test fixtures
*overengineering, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Reproduced: irispy SJI fixtures report slit x off-image (187 for 37-px-wide 2021 SJIs; 549-1268 for 178-px-wide 2023 SJIs), while a full local L2 SJI (400,417,388) gives 195.0, inside the frame. The fixtures look spatially decimated by 10 rather than cropped, since dividing by 10 puts both inside their frames. `pixel_stride` defaults to 1, so its production cost is one keyword. Dropping it, or keeping it test-only, is a minor simplification.
- **Recommendation:** Drop `pixel_stride` from the production API. Test the slit overlay on a full-resolution SJI, and accept or skip off-image fixture values in tests.
- **Evidence:** Scratch irispy probe: 'sns ...SJI_1400 (62, 40, 37) (187.0, 187.0)', 'raster ...20230408...SJI_1400 (2, 109, 178) (548.99, 1268.71)', 'FULL (400, 417, 388) 195.0 195.0'. wp4_common.py:16-22. Plan 791-793.

### followup-2-7 — WP2 moments block the GUI thread for seconds on sit-and-stare windows
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** MomentsDialog.run calls line_moments synchronously on the GUI thread, and WP2 has no responsiveness requirement. The measured blocking is 0.6 s on the 2018 windows and 2.2 s on sit-and-stare Si IV; about 4.7 s for Mg II k is an extrapolation. This is a single freeze during an explicit analysis action, not during browsing, so it is low severity for a quicklook. Moving it to the existing Worker, as WP6 does, is reasonable polish.
- **Recommendation:** WP2: run moments above about 5e7 elements in the existing Worker, as WP6 does, and add the maps on the main thread. Threshold: at most 0.5 s of GUI-thread blocking and a peak increase of at most 3x the float32 window bytes.
- **Evidence:** chk_wp2/wp2_moments_module.py:87 calls self.moments = line_moments(...) inside run() with no Worker or thread. Plan lines 721-743 give no timing requirement. s3_moments_SiIV1403.txt shows '0.6s on the calling (GUI) thread' with maxrss 2878029824, and s3_moments_sns_si.txt shows '2.2s ... rss_delta_after=2.74 GB maxrss=5.56 GB'.
### followup-2-9 — Raster/SJI loading still runs on the GUI thread; WP8 threads only the header scan
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** QtIRISImporter.finalize calls raster_data/image_data synchronously on the GUI thread and processes events only between picks. WP8 threads only the header scan. Measured warm-cache load times are 1.0-3.1 s per pick (the 99-file all-window stack was not run). The GUI is frozen during each pick.
- **Recommendation:** WP8/WP1: load in the existing Worker, with the progress bar showing while it runs and datasets added on the GUI thread. Threshold: loads over 1 s keep GUI timer gaps at most 0.2 s.
- **Evidence:** glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py:299-310@plan (processEvents at :302, raster_data at :306, image_data at :308). Plan lines 957-960 cover scanning only. s1_mg_stack.txt shows 't=2.74s', s1_all_stack_50.txt 't=3.13s', s2_quicklook.txt raster 't=1.00s' and SJI 't=1.60s', and s2_repeat_* SJI t=1.70-1.76s. After all my runs, 'git -C ~/Git/glue-solar status --short' printed nothing.

*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

### followup-3-7 — No SJI overlay of all raster slit positions; WP4 draws only the current frame's slit
*missing_requirement, confirmed*

- **Verified:** WP4 ports only a per-frame current-slit subset. Neither the plan, the prototypes nor stock glue offers a static overlay of all raster slit positions, which CRISPEX draws by default. SJI aux slit x cannot supply the positions (16 distinct over 32 frames against 64 steps). For dense rasters such an overlay mostly shows the field of view, and the moving current slit already gives the timing cue, so this is a parity gap for sparse rasters (IRIS-9's 4-step flare), not a blocker.
- **Recommendation:** WP4: add a toggleable static 'raster positions' overlay computed from the raster's step world coordinates and mapped into SJI pixels through WP1 link_hpc/WCS. Keep the per-frame current-slit subset as the timing indicator.
- **Evidence:** IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/wp4_common.py:16-33; plan:426 and 788-794. crispex-replay/results_iii.jsonl step 07a (mask_columns [650], slit_x 649.89, distinct 16, n_frames 32) and 07b (no API). iris9.txt:117-118 ('4 raster positions superimposed'); boulder.txt:127-130.
### followup-3-8 — Derived products cannot be exported usefully (moment maps lose WCS/Time; x–t diagrams are not Data)
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Confirmed: the glue FITS exporter writes a WCS header only for astropy WCS and skips non-numerical components, so a _GlueWCS moment map exports without WCS or Time. HDF5, FITS Table and VOTable fail on datetime64, and CSV and IPAC reject >1D data. WP2/WP6 have no export item. However, derived maps persist inside glue through WP3 sessions, and the plan already records that the PV tool is a legacy standalone widget and points to PathSlicedData for later slit work (plan:497, 617-619). Moments are declared non-prerequisite extensions (plan:462-463).
- **Recommendation:** Add an export acceptance item to WP2/WP6 and to the later slit work: a glue-solar data_exporter that writes the raw FITS/gWCS header of _GlueWCS and converts Time to ISO or MJD. Make the path extraction produce a Data (core PathSlicedData) so it can be saved, exported and restored.
- **Evidence:** .venv glue/core/data_exporters/gridded_fits.py:52, 61-63. crispex-replay/results_iii.jsonl step 18: FITS 'HDUs=[INTENSITY,...] CTYPE1=None', HDF5 'No conversion path for dtype <M8[ns]', VO Table TypeError; LaTeX Table 'ok' (omitted by the finding). Step 17x: PV adds 0 datasets. WP2 brief plan:721-742 has no export item.

### followup-3-10 — The raster cursor readout prints (lat, lon), unlabeled, where IRIS-9 asks for solar (x, y)
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** The readout on a raster map is '82" 112" (world)': latitude then longitude, unlabeled, in all tiers. This is WCSAxes format_coord following IRIS's WCS world order (wave, HPLT, HPLN). The precision is not fixed at whole arcsec: astropy formats with the current tick spacing (_fl_spacing), so it depends on zoom. Labelling or reordering is a valid low-priority Qt #74/WP1 item.
- **Recommendation:** WP1 or Qt #74: label the components (for example Solar X/Y or HPLN/HPLT) or order them lon/lat, and show sub-arcsec precision. Add a raster-map readout test.

*missing_requirement, confirmed*

*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

### followup-3-13 — WP4 sync: the raster-map viewer cannot drive time, and offsets are computed but not shown
*prototype_not_useful, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The WP4 prototype deliberately ignores any source viewer whose x or y axis is a leading (step/scan) axis, so a raster map or stack map viewer never drives SJI time. SJI and spectrogram stepping do sync. The signed offset (-70 ms) is computed by the test, but the prototype shows it nowhere. The plan already requires visible offsets (plan:437, 591-593), so this is a prototype gap against an existing requirement plus an unspecified map-to-frame rule.
- **Recommendation:** The WP4 production brief should define how a map's scan maps to an SJI frame (for example scan start or the step under the lock) and show the signed offset in the frame-time readout.
