- **Verified:** The derived iris_l2_20140910_fexxi_rb_steps file (PRIMARY/FEXXI_DATA/AUX, INSTRUME=SPEC, TELESCOP=IRIS) passes _is_supported_file. It is listed as observation 3860259453 with window 'O I 1356' and 1 raster file. Loading it fails at irispy spectrograph.py:189 (hdulist[-2] is FEXXI_DATA, which has no TIME keyword), and the dialog reports 'Loading O I 1356 failed: ...'. If the real 3860259453 rasters sat in the same folder, this file would probably join their raster list and break their load too; I could not test this without those files.
- **Recommendation:** WP8: mark files without the L2 raster layout as unsupported or derived instead of offering a window, or catch the error with a clear message. Add a test using the real header layout.
- **Evidence:** My probe: rb_steps layout hdus [PRIMARY, FEXXI_DATA, AUX], NWIN 1, TDESC ['O I 1356'], extra RSTEP0/RSTEP1/YPIX0/YPIX1; scan gave obsid 3860259453, windows ['O I 1356'], rasters 1; raster_data raised KeyError "Keyword 'TIME' not found." The finder's traceback shows spectrograph.py line 189. main conftest.py:168-174 writes only a sparse header, and test_scan.py:53-56 checks only the description.
### followup-4-7 — Main's wrapper with #2595 suggests a WCS link that then fails
*plan_incorrect, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** Reproduced: with main's wrapper plus core-combined, a rolled SJI paired with a sunpy make_fitswcs_header map gets 1 suggested autolink. SJI-to-map ROI then raises the latitude-range ValueError, and map-to-SJI selects 0 of 57820 pixels, while WP1's wrapper matches both ways. The finding misstates the plan, though. The table (546-553) makes no claim about SJI/sunpy-map pairs, and lines 554-557 already describe a latitude-range error from main's wrapper, which WP1 fixes. The real gap is that WP1's sunpy-map pairing tests (716-718) do not say to apply the links and check ROI propagation.
- **Recommendation:** WP1 acceptance test: apply every suggested autolink and check ROI propagation for a rolled SJI against a sunpy-style map, instead of only counting links.
- **Evidence:** My rerun of test_grid.py (main solar root + core-combined): rolled autolink n_links=1 -> 'ValueError: Latitude angle(s) must be within -90 deg <= angle <= 90 deg, got -41.927...'; reverse MISMATCH [(0,57820)x3]; decon25 0 links; NEG raster 1 link, match both ways. The finder's grid.jsonl shows wp1-core with 0 mismatches.
### followup-4-8 — The WP4 quicklook treats AIA cutouts as SJIs
*missing_requirement, partially_confirmed*

- **Verified:** wp4_common.quicklook:178 does classify any dataset with a 'time' physical type as an SJI, and AIA cutouts have [lon, lat, time]. Their OBSID ('20250519_165924_3640107442') reduces to the IRIS OBSID, so they would be time-linked to a matching raster. That nearest-frame AIA link is arguably useful context rather than a defect. A zero slit only appears if add_slit is applied to an AIA cube: quicklook never calls add_slit, but its guard would not stop it, because irispy's AIACube also carries a (zero) 'slit x position'. An AIA cutout becomes the first image viewer only when no SJI is selected, since the browser picks SJIs before AIA.
- **Recommendation:** Classify by INSTRUME or cube class (SJICube vs AIACube). WP4 test: a mixed SJI+AIA+raster selection pairs only the SJI and never adds a slit to AIA.
- **Evidence:** Headers: every AIA file in the 3640107442 _SDO folder has SLTPX1IX/SLTPX2IX all zero (357/357 for 171), and TIME ranges from -590.965 to 3681.034 s. My probe gives aia171 physical types [lon, lat, time]. Code: irispy sji.py:210 (adds slit x position for both classes), :239 (AIACube chosen by INSTRUME), wp4_common.py:22-28 (add_slit guard) and 157-166 (observation_key split on '_').
### followup-4-9 — F116: multi-exposure rasters make the step inverse ambiguous (not a blocker)
*other, confirmed*

- **Verified:** All local rasters with NEXP_PRP>1 are sit-and-stare (4000255147: 1600; 3660259102: 1020), as is the irispy 20210905 fixture (1872). On the synthetic NEXP_PRP=2 raster, times ascend. World-to-pixel returns the first exposure of each position (0, 2, ..., 14), so a step-0 box selects 0 grid pixels and a step-1 box selects 50, while a grid box selects 601 pixels on all 16 steps. I could not locate the 'F116' inventory row in the repo or scratch.
- **Recommendation:** Document the degenerate step inverse. Add a WP1 test with a duplicated-position raster checking that grid-to-raster selection covers both exposures.
- **Evidence:** My header scan of ~/DATA/IRIS/*_raster gives NEXP_PRP 1 for every scanning raster, and 1600/1020 only for STEPS_AV=0 sit-and-stare. irispy/data/test/{sns,raster}/iris_l2_20210905..._r00000.fits: NEXP_PRP 1872, STEPS_AV 0.0. Rerunning nexp_probe.py (WP1, released core) gives 'world->pixel step ... [0. 2. 4. 6. 8. 10. 12. 14.]', box (-0.5,0.5) -> 0, box (0.5,1.5) -> 50, and grid box -> [601 x16]. `grep -rn F116` finds nothing.
### followup-4-10 — Recorded Qt/core reproduction exports have been purged
*plan_stale, confirmed*

- **Verified:** The recorded reproduction roots under /tmp/iris-plan-validation-20260922 are effectively purged. core-profile and qt-profile hold 0 files and core-ape14 holds only glue/core/link_manager.py, but the directory skeletons remain. The empty qt-profile/glue_qt tree then shadows the installed glue_qt as a namespace package, so the documented commands fail with an ImportError. core-combined is complete against acaf4e1c.
- **Recommendation:** Re-export before re-running (the git archive recipes are documented), or run core-combined with the installed glue-qt as this review did.
- **Evidence:** `find ... -type f | wc -l`: core-ape14 1, core-profile 0, qt-profile 0, timeseries-deps 19. Importing with the qt-profile root on PYTHONPATH gives "ImportError: cannot import name 'ImageViewer' from 'glue_qt.viewers.image' (unknown location)" and glue_qt _NamespacePath(['/tmp/.../qt-profile/glue_qt']). `git ls-tree -r acaf4e1c` lists 485 files, and 0 are missing from core-combined. The README references are at review_20260923/README.md:99,107 and README.md:142-143. `git -C ~/Git/glue-solar status --short` is clean after my runs.

## Refuted findings
- core-qt-capabilities-7 Stock Navigate ignores image viewers opened after it is activated — In the GUI, Navigate re-collects its target viewers on every mouse press, so a later-opened Image viewer is driven from the next click or drag. The stale cache matters only if code sets nav_mode.state.x without a press (for example a WP4 controller), or for the brief gap before the next press. The reviewer's probe called _on_nav_activate once and then set x programmatically, skipping the press path.
- core-qt-capabilities-10 Roadmap omits the existing NDData translators in glue-astronomy — Plan line 1052 says generic NDCube/NDData and DKIST loaders are unplanned, and that is accurate. The roadmap item glue #2164 was 'Add NDData support as data factories', and the plan says loaders. glue-astronomy's @data_translator(NDDataArray/CCDData) handlers convert objects already in memory; the finding itself notes they are not file loaders, and NDCube is not handled. At most this is optional context, not an error in the plan.
- wp4-quicklook-6 Whisker preset uses the raster step axis for 4D stacks, contradicting the plan's time–wavelength definition — The brief defines whisker literally as 'wavelength versus step/time with a slit slider' (plan:805), and the prototype does exactly that: step × wavelength at a fixed slit, with scan left as a slider on 4D stacks. The plan's scan × wavelength time–wavelength view is a separate brief item (plan:816-818, 487, 502-508), not the whisker. So there is no contradiction. The only real gap is that the brief leaves the 4D whisker unspecified; the missing time–wavelength preset is covered by findings 7 and 13.
- usefulness-20 The WP1 'port the delta' instruction does not list the delta — The diff facts hold: 4 files differ, 2 exist only in WP1, and loaders/iris.py has +107/-16. The WP1 brief does list the delta, however. 'Primary paths' names `loaders/iris.py`, `sources/iris.py`, package setup and importer/linking tests (693-694). The checklist enumerates the functions: canonical names/Angstrom/high-level coherence (702-705), `link_hpc` plus browser call and menu action (706-707), and the gated inverse workaround (709-711). Only glue_patches.py is described rather than named by file.

### Data input, formats & large-data performance

| fid | feature | IRIS | status | priority | gap | home |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F020 | CRISPEX entry-point keyword set | y | exists_needs_preset_or_docs | could | Glue has no single 'open this IRIS observation as a quicklook' entry point. `glue raster.fits` loads every window of the file as a separate dataset and opens no layout. Keyword mapping: imcube/refcube become raster datasets, sjicube becomes SJI, LINE_CENTER becomes the WP5 rest wavelength and DT becomes the Time component. LAST_SESSION is F176; MASKCUBE, SCALE_STOKES, NO_WARP and SPECTFILE have no IRIS need. | WP4 (optional startup_action/menubar entry that opens the quicklook preset) + WP9 docs |
| F021 | Main image cube input (imcube) | y | exists_in_glue | must | Level-2 SJI and raster files load today as single scans, separate repeated scans, a 4D stack or a sit-and-stare cube. Level-3 and legacy binary files are not read, which is intended. Reads are eager (memmap=False at loaders/iris.py:141,147,174), so the 'never fully in memory' property is not met; see F034. | Existing loader (regression maintenance); lazy access under F034 |
| F022 | Spectral (transposed) cube input (spcube) | y | non_goal_in_plan | wont |  | non-goal (fast spectrum-time access is tracked as F035) |
| F023 | Data-cube ordering convention (folded third axis) | n | not_applicable_to_iris | wont |  | non-goal |
| F024 | FITS header parsing (axes, WCS, units, scaling, IRIS keywords) | y | exists_in_glue | must | Parsing is complete for Level-2, but wavelength is shown in metres. SJI and raster axis names also differ until WP1 lands. | irispy (parsing) + WP1 (names/units) |
| F025 | IRIS Level-3 im/sp FITS input and extensions | y | non_goal_in_plan | wont | Reading Level-3 is excluded, and this matches the settled decisions. Its extension data are covered by the Time component and the planned WP4 slit subset. | non-goal |
| F026 | Select which spectral windows to load or process | y | exists_in_glue | must | Windows are separate datasets rather than one concatenated spectral axis. This is acceptable because each keeps its own grid and scaling. File→Open (sources/iris.py:23-28) loads all windows with no choice. | Existing browser; WP9 docs |
| F027 | SOLARNET tabulated (-TAB) WCS support | n | not_applicable_to_iris | wont |  | non-goal (SST loader scope) |
| F028 | Legacy 'La Palma' CRISPEX binary cube format | n | not_applicable_to_iris | wont |  | non-goal |
| F029 | SINGLE_CUBE keyword | n | not_applicable_to_iris | wont |  | non-goal |
| F030 | Height-profile / simulation-cube mode | n | not_applicable_to_iris | wont |  | non-goal |
| F031 | SPECTFILE normalised/average spectrum file | n | not_applicable_to_iris | wont |  | non-goal |
| F032 | SCALE_CUBES multiplicative factor | n | exists_in_glue | wont |  | none needed |
| F033 | Programmatic access to the browsed cube | y | exists_needs_preset_or_docs | should | Scripts can reach the arrays, world coordinates and times through the Terminal, but this is undocumented. There is no qglue parser or data_translator for irispy/NDCube objects (plan :1052 leaves generic NDCube loaders unplanned). | WP9 docs; optional glue-solar data_translator for irispy cubes |
| F034 | Memory-mapped lazy cube access | y | partially_planned | should | Only measurement is planned, with no lazy or memmapped loading path. irispy's memmap mode drops scaling and masks, so lazy access needs loader work beyond flipping a flag. | glue-solar loader after first-milestone measurement (core DaskComponent) |
| F035 | Fast spectrum-vs-time access via sp cube; deferred updates without it | y | planned | must | The WP4 preset and the fixed-index wiring from the selected point are pending. There is no deferred-update/throttle option if large memmaps lag while dragging; this is unmeasured (plan:455-456). | WP4 |
| F036 | Sit-and-stare chunking | y | gap_not_in_plan | could | No exposure-range or chunked loading of long sit-and-stare files: the whole file is read eagerly. Navigation inside a loaded series needs no chunking. | glue-solar loader option, only if first-milestone measurement shows memory/latency problems (tie to F034) |

## Review gaps noted by the completeness critic
- Combined link graph never resolved (the most important contradiction between areas). Three separate failures were found: wp1-links-1 (a WCSLink silently overrides link_hpc), wp4-quicklook-1 (WP4 time links make SJI Pixel -> raster spectrum raise IncompatibleAttribute) and wp4-quicklook-2 (results depend on link order). usefulness-1 (ship M0 before WP1) also conflicts with usefulness-2 (SJI->raster needs unreleased #2595). No area traced the cause to core link resolution, which is greedy: the first accessible link wins and is replaced only at strictly lower cost (/tmp/iris-plan-validation-20260923/core-combined glue/core/link_manager.py:54-94). No area tested a design that meets D1 (plan:629), #2595 and WP4 identity links (plan:656) together.
- Real-data scale was only partly measured. usefulness measured just one 400-step raster (3.17 GB of data, 6.67 GB max RSS) and one SJI (806 MB). The 4D stack path reads every scan eagerly (glue_solar/sources/loaders/iris.py:174, memmap=False) and then copies it into a tempfile memmap (stack_spectrograms.py:44-45), and that was never run on the local 99-file series ~/DATA/IRIS/f783c3f4...-iris_l2_20260209_215233_3602506433_raster (1.9G). The matched 1600-exposure sit-and-stare with its SJI 1400 (20130902_163935_4000255147) was never opened in the quicklook. WP4 components (8.5 B/element), WP2/WP6 full-window runtimes and session sizes were measured only on fixtures or subsampled data, and the plan's performance criterion (plan:453-456) still has no thresholds.
- Observation-type variety was not tested. The local headers include a 45 deg rolled SJI (13f53b02...20140919_051712_3860608353_SJI_2832, SAT_ROT 45.0001), an east-to-west raster (20250328_225628_3400109360, STEPS_AV -0.998, SUMSPAT 2), a SUMSPTRL=4 raster (20260209), deconvolved SJIs and a 20140910_fexxi_rb_steps product. All are in ~/DATA/IRIS (python header scan in this critic run). No area ran the loader, WP1 units/links, WP4 slit/time links or the WP8 browser on them. The inventory marks F053 (scan direction) and F054 (roll) as gap_not_in_plan.
- Inventory priorities conflict with the plan and with glue. F095 cursor lock is marked [must], but the plan makes hover follow/lock optional (IRIS_GLUE_GAP_PLAN.md:432-433). F061 IRIS_HISTO_OPT is marked [must]/gap_not_in_plan, but glue 1.27.0 already offers percentile levels [100, 99.5, 99, 95, 90, Custom] (.venv glue/viewers/image/state.py:537), so it is closer to exists_needs_preset. The 43 gap_not_in_plan rows and the must/should partially_planned rows (F050, F109, F164, F190, ...) have no proposed WP owner. Spot checks of F060 (no 'colorbar' in glue 1.27.0 or glue-qt origin/main) and F087 (fixed 500 ms timer at glue-qt origin/main data_slice_widget.py:81-82) agree with the inventory.
- Duplicate findings and conflicting sign conventions need reconciling in the report. Mg II k TWAVE offset: wp2-wp6-science reports a +16.1 km/s velocity shift, while wp3-wp5 reports a 'bias -16.30 km/s' for the same TWAVE 2796.20 vs vacuum 2796.352 difference. The report should state one convention (v = c(lambda - lambda0)/lambda0, vacuum wavelengths). The 'local and untracked' claim is reported five times (solar-main-2, upstream-state-4, plan-integrity-1, archive-trace-6, usefulness-9); 'Time (Utc)', the Doppler image, TWAVE, Qt #66 and the #2603/#73 line layers are each duplicated as well.
- The CRISPEX workflow was never replayed in glue. All CRISPEX runtime behaviour comes from source and docs: IDL was not run, and the slides, screenshots and videos were not viewed (inv-iris-ssw and inv-journey unreachable lists). The IRIS-9 CRISPEX task list (recovered via Wayback by usefulness) and the Boulder 2016 exercises #1-#13 were not executed step by step in glue. So most of the 37 exists_in_glue and 15 exists_needs_preset_or_docs statuses rest on code reading or small probes, not on the end-to-end journey.
- Export of derived data was not tested. Only session serialization was checked. No area tried glue's stock data exporters (FITS/HDF5) on a WP2 moment map, a WP6 fit map or a Qt slice-extraction x-t image carrying _GlueWCS coordinates. F178/F182/F185/F187 statuses are therefore unverified.
- Packaging, distribution and declared floors were not examined. glue-solar has never been released (https://pypi.org/pypi/glue-solar/json returns 404), yet pyproject.toml:60 advertises that PyPI URL, the only entry point is glue.plugins (pyproject.toml:66), and no console launcher exists. The declared floors (glue-core>=1.23.0 at pyproject.toml:19, glue-qt>=0.4.0, sunpy[map,net]>=6.0.0, python>=3.12) were never tested; every run used Python 3.14, astropy 8.0.1 and glue-core 1.27.0. The plan has no release WP: plan:340-347 covers only glue-qt conda Cocoa and defers the .app.
- Upstream API churn through private APIs was not inventoried. The live prototypes depend on private internals: wp5_linelist_mod.py:116-117 (ProfileViewer._layer_style_widget_cls), wp5_blink.py:103 (axes._composite), wp3_impl.py:65-68 (astropy SlicedLowLevelWCS._slices_array/_wcs) and WP4's app.new_data_viewer wrapper. Individual breakages were found (wp3-wp5-15, wp4-quicklook-9), but no area listed these dependencies or mapped each to a public registry, as D2 (plan:634-638) requires.
- Licensing and provenance were not examined. The CRISPEX repository has no license (gh api repos/grviss/crispex -> license null; top-level files are README.md, aux, crispex.pro, resources, setup.sh). Features such as TANAT fits, IRIS_HISTO_OPT or CRISPEX layouts must therefore be reimplemented from their descriptions, not ported into BSD-licensed glue-solar. IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/iris_lines.csv has no source citation (for example NIST or CHIANTI).
- Platform and GUI realism were not covered. Every run was offscreen on macOS, and latency was measured without a real display. Linux, the usual SSW/IRIS analysis platform, and Windows were not exercised, and the user excluded CI evidence. The macOS acceptance section (plan:328-347) was checked only for CI wording (archive-trace-15); nobody judged whether it belongs in a CRISPEX-focused plan.
- Some live entry points were not executed or were only mocked: the wp5_ll_session.py demo listed in the prototype index; WP7's real GOES/AIA download and crop (Fido searches only, plus a transform probe for the off-limb failure); and a WP8 scan of a realistic tree such as ~/DATA/IRIS (14G of extracted data and tarballs) for timing and stop latency.

## Claims verified correct
- solar-main: Solar suite on main reproduces 33 passed, 2 skipped with the two skip reasons the plan names (suite.log; test_plugin.py:93,143)
- solar-main: Plan branch code is identical to main 236f0a8; only the plan and prototypes differ
- solar-main: #44/#50/#52/#53 are merged on 2026-09-22 with the heads listed in the plan table (gh pr view)
- solar-main: SJI axes use gWCS names Longitude/Latitude/Time (UTC); raster names are filled from physical type (Wavelength, Helioprojective Latitude/Longitude) via _AXIS_NAMES (loaders/iris.py:27-43@236f0a8)
- solar-main: Helioprojective values are shown in arcsec (with longitude wrapping) and wavelength in metres ('m'); stack units are (m, arcsec, arcsec, '') (loaders/iris.py:45-69; test_importer.py:178)
- solar-main: Every SJI, raster and stack dataset has a datetime64[ns] DateTimeComponent 'Time'. AIA cutouts get it too through the gWCS path (probe; test_importer.py:136)
- solar-main: Stack dtype is np.result_type(first.dtype, float32), giving float32 for >f4 IRIS input. The stack is a temp-file np.memmap, masked pixels are set to NaN, the mask is ~isfinite, and scan 0's WCS is compounded with a 'Scan' axis without resampling (stack_spectrograms.py:42-83)
- solar-main: Unstacked reads are eager: read_files(..., memmap=False, uncertainty=False) (loaders/iris.py:141,147,174)
- solar-main: The solar:cursor_readout toolbar gate is hasattr(ImageViewer,'cursor_status') (__init__.py:19-23). cursor_status exists only on Qt #74 6b579814, not v0.4.2 or origin/main 9780eaf9. Nuance: the @viewer_tool registry entry is unconditional; only toolbar inclusion is gated
- solar-main: iris_image_layer sets cmap_bad=(0,0,0,0) only for image viewers and data with OBSID in meta (sources/iris.py:31-44). Subsets have no meta, so they are excluded; stack (dict meta) and AIA cutouts carry OBSID
- solar-main: Production IRIS sessions cannot be saved on glue-core 1.27.0, and save_session serializes before writing, so 'saving can fail before any file is written' is correct (probe_session2; application_base.py:101-125)
- solar-main: WP9 open items verified present: AMI alt-text (loading-aia-and-hmi.rst:43), the _SDO-folder sentence (loading-iris...rst:28), the restore-only session warning (:64), no float32/NaN text, no 52/53 fragments, a 0-byte CHANGELOG.rst, and whatsnew/changelog.rst rendering towncrier fragments
- solar-main: scan.py has no _SDO directory requirement: AIA cutouts are accepted by aia_l2_ name plus INSTRUME AIA*, anywhere in the walk (scan.py:110-115,134-137)
- solar-main: The docs Linking section tells users to pair 'Helioprojective Longitude/Latitude', which SJI lacks; WP9's distinct-labels bullet covers this
- solar-main: 93d05f05 is a commit; docs/make_screenshots.py there is 196 lines and regenerates the 8 profile-guide PNGs; it is on neither main nor in the prototypes; 11 commits are not on any remote
- solar-main: Raster WCS is FITS -TAB (HPLT-TAB/HPLN-TAB) and SJI is gwcs, so the plan's WP3/-TAB and gWCS statements hold
- solar-main: The WP1/WP8 primary paths exist (loaders/iris.py, sources/iris.py, scan.py, iris_loader.ui, test_importer.py, test_scan.py); glue_qt.utils.threading.Worker exists in v0.4.2; the directory walk is fully sorted before processing (scan.py:137)
- solar-main: Archive extraction is transactional through a temporary sibling directory plus rename (scan.py:29-51)
- solar-main: pytest.ini passes --doctest-rst and pytest-doctestplus is not installed; recorded environment versions match

- wp1-links: The WCS capability matrix reproduces (4 passed with main, 4 passed with WP1). Main gives 0 links for raster/raster, stack/scan and derived maps and raises the latitude-range error (-402.099 deg); WP1 gives 1 link for each.
- wp1-links: D3 holds for all IRIS WCS kinds in WP1: high-level round trips for the SJI gWCS, the raster FITS-TAB WCS, the compound stack and the sliced map; Angstrom wavelength (1398.68 A); callable and string getters; 3- and 4-tuple class specs (d3_probe.py).
- wp1-links: The link_hpc contract holds: it returns a list of LinkSame, is idempotent (a second call returns []), pairs by physical type, chains every dataset to the first one, and ignores degree-based sunpy maps (test_linking.py:82-136, passing).
- wp1-links: The inverse workaround's logic is identical to core #2598's closure. Its behaviour probe returns False on a #2598 export (no patch) and True on released 1.27.0. test_world_links fails when the patch is removed, so the test really covers it.
- wp1-links: The 42.2202 arcsec drift over 4.81 h reproduces (42.220225531861615), and it holds on irispy 0.9.0 fixtures as well.
- wp1-links: #2595's docstring states the link is 'exact at the first exposure' (core-ape14 wcs_autolinking.py:113), which supports D1's frame-0 framing.
- wp1-links: With #2595, raster/SJI -> sunpy-map autolinks already work and give correct pixel values on both main and WP1.
- wp1-links: No test run created files in glue-solar; git status is clean.
- wp2-wp6-science: The recorded WP2+WP6+integration command reproduces '17 passed' (5 WP2 + 8 WP6 + 4 integration tests) and creates no repo files.
- wp2-wp6-science: irispy 0.8.1 has calculate_moments(cube, *, rest_wavelength, wings, integrated, min_intensity, saturation_limit). It returns intensity/centroid/width in nm (and DN or DN nm), plus velocity/velocity_width in km/s when a rest wavelength is known (irispy/utils/moments.py:33-209).
- wp2-wp6-science: WP2 converts nm outputs to Angstrom and the integrated threshold to DN Angstrom correctly: test_integrated_zeroth_moment_is_dn_angstrom passes, and irispy converts Quantity thresholds at moments.py:163-166.
- wp2-wp6-science: The Doppler convention is consistent: irispy moments and red_blue use optical (λ-λ0)/λ0·c (moments.py:190-194, red_blue.py:214), WP5 uses u.doppler_optical (wp5_units.py:32), both give 10.7208 km/s at +0.1 Å, and positive means redshift.
- wp2-wp6-science: The parallel_fit_dask call (data, fitting_axes, world tuple, model, fitter, scheduler) matches the astropy 8.0.1 keyword-only signature. The astropy>=7.2 floor (from irispy-lmsal 0.8.1) guarantees it exists (added in 7.0), and dask[array] comes through irispy, so 'no dask/fitting extra' holds.
- wp2-wp6-science: glue_qt.utils.threading.Worker(func, *args) is a QThread with result/error signals. The processes scheduler inside it completed, with the progress dialog visible and a non-cancellable busy dialog as the plan says.
- wp2-wp6-science: The seeds match the irispy gallery: 0.005 nm (gallery 01 line 86), 0.008 nm, and 279.621/279.650 nm (gallery 07), so SPLIT_SEED 0.15 Å is half the separation.
- wp2-wp6-science: The plan's claim that glue Profile Collapse moments are in pixel units is correct (glue/core/aggregate.py:8-50), and glue has Gaussian/Polynomial fitters (glue/core/fitters.py:269, 348).
- wp2-wp6-science: The WP5 line list uses vacuum wavelengths consistent with irispy gallery 07 (279.6351 nm) and ITN 26 (O I 135.560 nm).
- wp2-wp6-science: CRISPEX has no moments or Gaussian fitting, so treating WP2/WP6 as extensions rather than first-milestone prerequisites (plan line 462) is correct.
- wp2-wp6-science: The WP6 _GlueWCS(SlicedLowLevelWCS(raw_wcs, slices)) contract round-trips through the WP3 prototype for both WP6 variants (test_plan_integration.py:44-71 pass).
- wp2-wp6-science: D4 holds in both prototypes: the actions add a dataset plus pixel links and open no viewer (wp2_moments_module.py:98-101, wp6_fitting.py:117-123).
- wp2-wp6-science: The WP2 prototype rejects SJI and 4D stacks, and WP6 rejects non-spectrogram data (tests pass).
- wp3-wp5: The recorded reproductions still pass: 15 passed (refresh helpers/blink, line positions, 6 WP3 session cases) and 3 passed on core #2601 + Qt #70 sources, matching IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/README.md:80-81 and plan lines 750-755.
- wp3-wp5: iris_lines.csv has exactly 11 lines (plan line 850). C II 1334.53/1335.71, Si IV 1393.76/1402.77, Mg II k/h 2796.35/2803.53, O I 1355.60, Cl I 1351.66 and O IV 1401.16 agree with NIST vacuum values within 0.005 Å. O IV 1399.77 is not in NIST ASD and was not independently verified.
- wp3-wp5: Doppler convention is consistent: wp5_units uses u.doppler_optical, v=c(λ-λ0)/λ0, the same formula as irispy/utils/moments.py:187-193 and red_blue.py:214.
- wp3-wp5: The stack TWAVE1 caveat (plan line 849) is valid: stack_spectrograms.py builds meta=dict(scan0.meta), and the probe showed stack meta is a plain dict with rest_wavelength None, while TWAVE1 is always the first (C II) window.
- wp3-wp5: Blink semantics are correctly scoped: CRISPEX spectral blink toggles two wavelength indices of one cube (crispex.pro:13859-13880), so plan line 449 is right that layer blink alone does not meet the criterion. Layer blink corresponds to CRISPEX main/reference blink (crispex.pro:4221-4230).

Declaring it is necessary: sunpy.timeseries fails to import without h5netcdf.

- followup-1: Precedence rule, identical in core-combined and released 1.27.0. discover_links (link_manager.py:54-94) starts with the dataset's own main and coordinate components at depth 0 (:71-75), so these are never overridden. Each pass takes the first link, in iteration order, whose inputs are all derivable (:38-51, :78). Its cost is max(input depth)+1 (:81-84), and it is kept only if it adds a new component or strictly lowers the depth (:85-89); then the scan restarts (:90). Result: each target gets its fewest-hop path; ties go to the first link met in the set built at :250/:268-288. Data._get_external_link (data.py:662-666) stores the single winner, which get_data (data.py:454-455), translate_pixel (fixed_resolution_buffer.py:82-99) and pixel_cid_to_pixel_cid_matrix (link_manager.py:420-440) all read.
- followup-1: Prior finding confirmed: WCSLink overrides link_hpc on pixel paths deterministically, by cost (1 hop vs 2), not by order. The winner for sji<-raster pixel0 is the WCSLink ComponentLink(Pixel Axis 2 [x], Pixel Axis 1 [y]); for the raster longitude it is still link_hpc's identity LinkSame (run_081.log baseline line).
- followup-1: Prior finding confirmed: WP4 time links make the SJI Pixel-to-raster marker/spectrum raise IncompatibleAttribute, in all 6 orders and on both irispy versions. The cause is the 'Nearest frame' link making SJI pixel 0 derivable on the raster (pixel_selection_subset_state.py:36-45).
- followup-1: D1 (keep link_hpc alongside APE-14 pixel links) needs no change. Designs B and C keep link_hpc and pass (a)-(e) in 6/6 orders on irispy 0.8.1 and 0.9.0, and link_hpc is what makes the cross-dataset world readouts in (d) correct (test_d_needs_hpc).
- followup-1: Plan:562-564 is correct: with #2595 plus link_hpc (and no pixel-targeting time links), an SJI Pixel subset yields a raster marker and spectrum via get_xy/to_array: baseline xy (24,3), spectrum (1,1,29) on irispy 0.8.1.
- followup-1: Plan:138 is correct: #2595 provides no time matching. Frame-to-step sync must come from nearest-index components or from the Time components (designs B and C both give 0/124 forward and 0/374 backward mismatches).
- followup-1: Each dataset's own world components are never changed by any design or order ('own world unchanged' True in every row), consistent with depth-0 native components in discover_links:71-75.
- followup-1: Design C (no time links; sync computed from the Time components by a callback) also passes 6/6, but it drops the plan:796-797 requirement that the links be visible and removable in the Link Editor. That makes B the better fit; B's 4D (scan, step) variant and multi-SJI/multi-raster collections were not tested.
- followup-2: WP4 nearest-index components cost no RSS: link_time for 2 rasters took 0.001 s with a 0.00 GB RSS delta and 16,000 B of backing arrays, because they are broadcast views (s2_quicklook.txt LAT link_time line).
- followup-2: Pixel click to computed and drawn spectrum is 0.278-0.331 s over 9 clicks in 3 runs, on 1.75e8- and 3.70e8-element sit-and-stare windows; every spectrum point is finite (s2_quicklook, s2_repeat_1/3).
- followup-2: quicklook() opens its three viewers in 1.4-2.1 s, and the first draw takes 0.16 s on real sit-and-stare plus SJI data.
- followup-2: The stack temp file is float32 and sized to memmap nbytes: lsof shows an unlinked 231,384,384 B file in TMPDIR, matching the plan's line 361 dtype claim.
- followup-2: The WP6 Worker keeps the GUI responsive: max 20 ms-timer gap of 0.086-0.095 s during 18.7-20.9 s full-window fits. Timings match the recorded IRIS_PLAN_PROTOTYPES/wp6_full_raster.log (24.6-25.3 s) and NaN centroids stay at 0.00-0.02%.
- followup-2: WP8 scan is fast and stop latency is bounded by a single header read (max 165.6 ms, on the SJI .fits.gz; observed stop wait of at most 150.8 ms). This is consistent with the plan's note that 'header reads can delay stop' and the wp8_scan.py:4-5 claim that scanning multi-GB data takes seconds.
- followup-2: Core #2595 WCSLink between the real SJI and the 1600-exposure raster evaluates quickly (0.009 s per 100 SJI pixels), so APE-14 pixel links are not a measured scale problem.
- followup-2: WP2 moments on 2018 windows complete in 0.6 s with fully finite intensity maps; only the sit-and-stare size is a responsiveness issue.
- followup-2: The task's cited lines are accurate: iris.py:174 reads with memmap=False, and stack_spectrograms.py:44-45 creates the TemporaryFile memmap.
